# 490d State Vocabulary Register v1.55

## 0. File-function

This Register is the single active home for per-file modal-state vocabulary, per-file convention updates, and per-file Machine Guards that were previously accreted into the 490d Repository Style Guide. The Style Guide v2.5 retains generic, repository-wide rules and the generic glossary entries (former §31.1–§31.37); this Register holds everything file-specific.

Numbering from the Style Guide v2.4 source is preserved exactly so that any existing cross-reference of the form `Style Guide §31.x` or `Style Guide §32x` resolves here without renumbering. A reference to `Style Guide v2.4 §31.139` is now read `State Vocabulary Register §31.139`.

Control status: the entries in Parts A, B, and C are active controls, equal in authority to their former placement in the Style Guide. The Repository_Change_Archive holds historical records only and is non-controlling.

When new files are finalized, their state vocabulary, convention updates, and Machine Guards register here. The Style Guide and Project Procedures receive at most a one-line pointer.

## 0.1 File map

| Part | Function | Source migrated from |
|---|---|---|
| Part A | Per-file modal-state glossary entries §31.38–§31.167 | Style Guide v2.4 §31 |
| Part B | Per-file convention updates and Machine Guards §32A–§32U | Style Guide v2.4 §32A–§32U |
| Part C | File_36, File_41, File_45 glossary additions and Machine Guards | Style Guide v2.4 update-record subsections |
| Part D | Post-migration file registrations | Post-final file-specific entries |

Machine Guard [REGISTER MIGRATION FIDELITY]:
All entries below are verbatim relocations from Style Guide v2.4. Do not treat relocation as revision. If an entry appears to conflict with a repository file, resolve it exactly as it would have been resolved when the entry lived in the Style Guide.

# Part A. Per-File Modal-State Glossary (migrated Style Guide §31.38–§31.167)

### 31.38 Three-Gear Protocol state

A File_11 state in which Gear 1 / Japheth, Gear 2 / Shem, and Gear 3 / Ham function as simultaneous Gear settings under Shem Superposition. Gear 1 is `+0`, Gear 2 is `+2`, and Gear 3 is `+4` from Gear 1 / `+2` from Gear 2. These settings are not corrections of one another. The active Gear, figure, operator, and dependency must be stated before applying a Gear shift.

### 31.39 Flood-Gear rail state

The File_11 / File_00 rail field containing `2456 BC`, `2458 BC`, and `2460 BC`. These are distinct Gear rail states and must not be collapsed into one Flood date or one manuscript chronology. In File_02 terminology, `2458 BC` remains the MT Standard-Normal / Gear-2 / Shem-axis Flood state unless a local file explicitly opens another rail.

### 31.40 Filling Protocol state

A File_11 state mapping cumulative Kenan / Cainan coordinates to Creation-week Days 4 / 6 / 7 and to Gear 3 / Gear 2 / Gear 1. Its default scope is the local cumulative Kenan / Cainan field. Do not generalize the Filling Protocol to unrelated Creation-week, Flood, or cumulative fields unless a controlling dependency explicitly broadens the scope.

### 31.41 Source-history biographical shorthand

A legacy or source-history biographical label retained for traceability without becoming an active confirmed chronological state. Example: `Noah 504?` in File_11 is source-history shorthand only. It must not be promoted to a confirmed biographical state unless a controlling dependency defines that state.

### 31.42 Priestly 336-day Ledger state

A foundational Priestly service-cycle architecture generated by `24 × 7 = 168`, `168 + 168 = 336`, and `48 × 7 = 336`. The execution-variant invariant `24 × 14 = 336` may be noted where relevant. This state is not a failed solar year and not a replacement for the 360-day Prophetic Year or the 364-day Enochian Year.

### 31.43 24-course roster source-control state

A source-control state grounded in the 24 priestly courses of 1 Chronicles 24:1–19, with associated weekly service, Sabbath transition, New Testament continuity, Josephus, Mishnah, and Qumran Mishmarot witnesses. It supports the Priestly 336-day Ledger architecture but does not by itself determine every historical execution detail.

### 31.44 Seven-day service-period state

The Priestly service-period state in which a course serves for seven days. The standard source-control anchor is 1 Chronicles 9:25. It supports `24 × 7 = 168` when the 24 courses are treated as a half-year service ledger.

### 31.45 Sabbath transition-boundary state

The weekly transition boundary between outgoing and incoming priestly divisions, source-controlled by 2 Kings 11:5–7 and 2 Chronicles 23:4–8. It supports the Sabbath-to-Sabbath service-cycle architecture without requiring every local file to reconstruct temple-roster administration.

### 31.46 Priestly-Enochian bridge corollary

A corollary state in which four festival or epagomenal weeks bridge the 336-day Priestly Ledger to the 364-day Enochian Year: `4 × 7 = 28`; `336 + 28 = 364`. It does not erase the 336-day baseline and should not be treated as one mandatory historical mechanism unless a local file supplies source-control.

### 31.47 Priestly-Enochian half-week straddle

A File_12 dependency state in which the Priestly weekly vector and the Enochian solar Creation-week vector produce a structural `3.5`-day liturgical split. Under the day-year principle, this may project as `3.5 × 360 = 1260` and compare with `3.5 × 364 = 1274`. Do not generalize this state to every `3.5` value unless the active file opens that operator.

### 31.48 Source-control bibliography vector

A compact source-control list attached to a state or operator. For the Priestly 336-day Ledger, the vector includes 1 Chronicles 24:1–19, 1 Chronicles 9:25, 2 Kings 11:5–7, 2 Chronicles 23:4–8, Luke 1:5, Josephus Antiquities 7.365–366, Mishnah Taanit 4:2, Mishnah Sukkah 5:7–8, Qumran Mishmarot texts such as 4Q320–4Q330, and secondary scholarship such as Talmon, VanderKam, Elior, Ben-Dov, and Ratzon / Johnson. The vector supports source-control; it does not replace local arithmetic or modal-state discipline.

### 31.49 Christological joint-file state

A file-state in which a repository file gathers Christ-centered articulation points from multiple domains rather than proving one isolated chronology. `File_13` is the current example. It touches genealogy, Creation-week, Flood-week, Conquest, Incarnation, Passion, Temple, LXX, and Mirror states. A Christological joint file may index dependency-controlled results from other files, but it must not silently convert them into local proofs.

### 31.50 Second-Adam / Jan-1 covenant numeric-year Mirror

A File_13 local covenant Mirror label tied to the Jan 1, AD 1 naming / circumcision hinge. It may display readable labels such as `5321 BC ↔ AD 5321` and `2801 BC ↔ AD 2801`. This is not ordinary elapsed civil-span arithmetic and not automatically Protocol 1. Executable cross-axis spans still use `BC + AD − 1` unless a local Mirror protocol is explicitly opened.

### 31.51 File_13 Passion-date pair state

A Christological Passion-date pair state. `AD 33n` is the preferred Friday Nisan `14/15` Passion state. `AD 30n` remains a possible Friday Nisan `14/15` Passion state. `Nisan 14/15` remains a Gospel-calendar slash-pair. The non-Passion display members are handled separately under the AD 33n / AD 30n / AD 33t / AD 34 display-state firewall.

### 31.52 File_13 day-year purification display

A birth-of-Christ day-year state in which Dec 25, 1 BC is Day -6, the inclusive eighth-day circumcision reaches Jan 1, AD 1, and the traditional 40th-day purification reaches symbolic AD 33 by 39 full days. AD 34 may be retained only as a completed-period / exclusive-end purification display state. AD 34 is not a Passion-date state.

### 31.53 Matthew exile-bearing Abraham head bracket

A File_13 / File_43 Matthean head-envelope state generated by the overlapped exile-bearing generation. The head bracket is `2876 BC`, with phase-expanded display `2877t/2876n BC → 2870t/2869n BC` where exact Tishri/Nisan phase work is active. It is not Abraham's literal birth and must remain distinct from the ordinary Matthean Abraham anchor `2806 BC`.

### 31.54 Cumulative MT Shem / Noah Mirror state

A File_13 cumulative-MT Mirror coordinate-completion state for `5436 BC ↔ AD 5436`. In this state, `5436 BC` is the cumulative MT birth of Shem / death of Noah field, 600 years before the `4836–4829 BC` Shem-death / Flood / Arphaxad-birth field. The `536 BC/AD` pivot display is a dual-side 4900-year display, not the direct civil midpoint of `5436 BC → AD 5436`.

### 31.55 AD 33n / AD 30n / AD 33t / AD 34 display-state firewall

A master node-class firewall for File_13-related Christological date labels. `AD 33n` is the preferred Friday Nisan `14/15` Passion state. `AD 30n` is a possible Friday Nisan `14/15` Passion state. `AD 33t` is the Tishri-side completion member and must not be treated as a Tishri / Tabernacles crucifixion. AD 34 is a completed-period purification display state or, if separately opened as AD 34/35, an Acts expansion field. Do not collapse these labels into one Passion date and do not treat non-Passion display members as crucifixion dates.

### 31.56 File_13 LXX actual / rounded firewall

A state-control firewall distinguishing File_13's LXX actual Creation endpoint and Year-6 states from File_54's rounded comparison states. In `File_13`, `5494 BC` is the LXX actual Creation endpoint, `5496 BC` is the LXX Year-6 state, and `5526 BC` is the LXX Year-6 +30 Apparent Age state. These do not include `+60 Terah` and must not be replaced by File_54's rounded `5486 BC`, rounded +30 `5516 BC`, or Year-6 +60 `5556 BC` states.

### 31.57 Phase-resolved paired Mirror coordinate-completion

A Mirror coordinate-completion state for paired Tishri/Nisan dates. The controlling display rule is `Yt/(Y−1)n BC → AD (Y−1)t/Y n` when exact `.5` phase-pair work is opened. This is distinct from a single-date numeric-year Mirror node and distinct from ordinary civil cross-axis span-counting.

### 31.57a File_43 Luke / Matthew Genealogical Bridge state

A File_43 state in which Luke and Matthew are harmonized through a locked 70-year carrier while preserving mandatory split states. It does not collapse LukeSpan with LukeIntervals, does not collapse MattSpan_BirthHinge with MattSpan_Carrier, and does not chain the P2 rails-window with the A-space bridge.

### 31.57b LukeSpan / LukeIntervals split state

A File_43 split between `LukeSpan = 5320 = 76 × 70`, the names-span from Adam to Christ as names, and `LukeIntervals = 5250 = 75 × 70`, the interval-count between the 76 names. Use the label required by the local calculation.

### 31.57c MattSpan_BirthHinge / MattSpan_Carrier split state

A File_43 split between `MattSpan_BirthHinge = 2800 = 40 × 70`, the Abraham to `6 BC` bracket, and `MattSpan_Carrier = 2870 = 41 × 70`, the Abraham to AD 65 carrier bracket. Bare `Matthew span` should not be used where the distinction matters.

### 31.57d Genealogical MirrorVolume state

A File_43 span-provenance volume state defined as `8050 = LukeIntervals 5250 + MattSpan_BirthHinge 2800`. It is not formed with MattSpan_Carrier and is not a formal Mirror protocol unless a local section explicitly opens such a protocol.

### 31.57e Seal700 state

A File_43 expansion seal generated by the ordinary Key-of-23 whole-year expansion: `8050 × 25/23 = 8750`, and `8750 − 8050 = 700 = 10 × 70`. It is not an independent genealogy span.

### 31.57f Display-table-only genealogy ledger

A File_43 display state in which the Luke / Matthew 70-year genealogy ledger is a mechanized ledger for visualization and comparison. It must not overwrite LukeSpan, LukeIntervals, MattSpan_BirthHinge, MattSpan_Carrier, MirrorVolume, expanded volume, or Seal700.

### 31.57g Figurative God bookend label state

A File_43 / File_54 display-label state in which `God` refers figuratively to Luke's `Adam, son of God` endpoint and to Christ the Son of God as a theological bookend. It must not be read as divine origination, a literal birth chronology for God, or a Matthean textual datum.

### 31.57h Double-generational knot compositional state

A File_43 compositional state in which an end-cap or middle generation may be overlapped for literary or typological framing. It is not an arithmetic input and must not replace the locked span labels.

### 31.57i Job-only restoration warrant state

A File_43 source-control state narrowing the double-generational-knot warrant to Job as a typological / compositional restoration analogy. Job's restored `140` years may be read typologically as `2 × 70`, with a doubled-life or four-generation restoration horizon, but this does not create a local chronology proof about Job. Unclear secondary warrants should be removed or marked audit-controlled unless locally sourced.

### 31.57j Outer Luke rail / overlapped middle-generation state

A File_43 / File_54 dependency-controlled state in which the outer Luke rail `5356 BC → AD 5355` gives `10710 = 153 × 70`, and adding the overlapped middle Christ-generation gives `10780 = 154 × 70 = 22 × 490`. This explains the `153 + 1` display without altering File_43's local span locks.

### 31.57k Alpha-Omega 22-fold bracket state

A File_43 / File_54 typological and reception-history state in which `22 × 490 = 22 × 7 × 70` frames the start, central Christ-generation, and terminal rail as an Alpha-Omega / beginning-middle-end display. This is not a local arithmetic driver and does not prove direct Lukan, Johannine, or BJ dependence.

### 31.57l Revelation Alpha-Omega reception-history note

A claim-status note for using Revelation 1:8, Revelation 21:6, and Revelation 22:13 as Alpha-Omega / Beginning-End / First-Last framing. It supplies theological / typological / reception-history context, not independent chronological derivation.

### 31.57m BJ / Jubilees 22-fold Creation-genealogy dependency state

A dependency-controlled state in which File_53's BJ / Jubilees 22-fold Creation-genealogy witness is cited through File_54 to support the symbolic fit of a 22-fold bracket. It does not import the full File_53 proof burden into a local file unless explicitly opened.

### 31.57n File_43 P2 File_32 routing state

A File_43 final dependency-routing state in which active P2 rails-window and A-space bridge handling routes through bounded File_32 terminology: `36 BC ↔ 6 BC` belongs to the P2 rails-window, and `6 BC ↔ AD 25` belongs to the P2 signed A-space bridge. Do not revive the removed active File_42 dependency label for File_43.

Machine Guard [FILE_43 SPAN SPLIT]:
Do not use `LukeSpan` where File_43 requires `LukeIntervals`. Do not use `MattSpan_Carrier` where File_43 requires `MattSpan_BirthHinge`. Bare `Matthew span` is insufficient where the distinction matters.

Machine Guard [FILE_43 P2 NON-STRADDLING]:
Do not chain `36 BC ↔ 6 BC ↔ AD 25` as one continuous operator. The `36 BC ↔ 6 BC` relation belongs to the P2 rails-window, and `6 BC ↔ AD 25` belongs to the P2 signed A-space bridge.

Machine Guard [FILE_43 ALPHA-OMEGA NON-DRIVER]:
The Alpha-Omega `22 × 490` note is theological / typological / reception-history framing. It is not a new local arithmetic operator, not a new Mirror protocol, not a substitute for File_43's genealogy locks, and not proof of direct textual dependence unless a later source-critical dependency explicitly opens that claim.

### 31.58 Biological-fractal state

A File_14 state in which biological, gestational, purification, and numerical structures are read as coordinated micro-chronology controls. The principal values are `270`, `275`, `276`, and `280`, but each value retains its own node-class and operator.

### 31.59 Variant-gap gestation state

A File_14 state in which the manuscript variant gap functions as a gestational structure. `275` is the variant gap; `270` and `280` arise by diagonal measurement; `276` arises only when the full Creation-year boundary extension is included.

### 31.60 Sequential variant-gap anatomy

The decomposed File_14 display `60 + 155 + 60 = 275`. It names the sequential anatomy of the variant field and must not be collapsed into the compressed boundary display without restating the active state.

### 31.61 Compressed boundary display

The File_14 display `60 + 215 = 275`. It is a compressed boundary form used in Triple-1656 contexts. It preserves the same `275` gap but does not replace the sequential anatomy `60 + 155 + 60 = 275`.

### 31.62 `276` full-year boundary-extension state

A File_14 boundary-envelope state generated by adding one full Creation year to the `275` variant gap. The controlling Year-6 example is `4176n BC → 3901n BC = 275`; `3901n BC → 3900n BC = 1`; therefore `4176n BC → 3900n BC = 276`. This preserves `275` as the variant gap and permits the exact conversion `276 × 70/69 = 280`.

### 31.63 Triple-1656 Spine

A File_14 structural state in which LXX Maximum, MT Minimum, Flood, and Fall-of-Jerusalem date-states align through repeated `1656` spans. This state must preserve its active endpoint and boundary labels, especially `5555 BC`, `5554 BC`, `3899 BC`, `3898 BC`, `2243 BC`, `2242 BC`, `587 BC`, and `586 BC`.

### 31.64 Typological mirroring

A reciprocal or reflected typological structure that uses mirror-like language without opening formal repository Mirror target-generation. In File_14, the `40 + 40` purification material uses typological mirroring / reciprocal-structure language, not File_00 foundational Mirror protocol.

### 31.65 LXX Maximum node-label state

A File_14 state separating the LXX Maximum Creation labels by node-class: `5561 BC` as Creation Week opening / Year-1 display; `5560 BC` as Year-1-end display; `5556 BC` as Year-6 / Adam-state display; `5555 BC` as Year-7-start boundary; and `5554 BC` as endpoint / Year-7-end display. Do not collapse these labels into one LXX Maximum date.

### 31.66 `587/586 BC` adjacent Fall of Jerusalem date states

A File_14 date-pair state in which `587 BC` is an allowed Fall of Jerusalem date and the local exact Triple-1656 terminus under Year-7-boundary reckoning, while `586 BC` remains the primary repository Fall of Jerusalem / schematic endpoint state. The label `587 BC` must not be treated as merely a non-historical boundary short of Jerusalem's fall.

### 31.67 Precessional-day component-display convention

A File_14 display convention for precessional tables in which an added year value is first stated arithmetically and then expressed in standard 72-year precessional-day units. Examples: `144 = 72 × 2 = 2 precessional days`; `432 = 72 × 6 = 6 precessional days`; `72 = 72 × 1 = 1 precessional day`. This is a display convention, not a Residue Protocol and not ordinary elapsed-day arithmetic.


### 31.68 Authorized generational-unit state

File_15's state in which Abrahamic `100`, Jacobean `70`, and Mosaic `40` function as authorized generational units. These values must not be treated as generic interchangeable component values unless the File_15 state is active.

### 31.69 Call-to-death generational-span register

File_15's authorization rationale in which Abraham `75 → 175 = 100`, Jacob `77 → 147 = 70`, and Moses `80 → 120 = 40` define the three authorized generational units. The exact spans are dependency-controlled arithmetic facts; the unifying rationale is structural inference.

### 31.70 Exact normalized generational triad

The exact File_15 ratio state `100:70:40 = 10:7:4`. This form replaces proof-bearing decimal approximation in generational-unit comparisons.

### 31.71 Abrahamic-base generational fractions

The File_15 fractional display `10/10`, `7/10`, and `4/10 = 2/5`, using the Abrahamic `100` as base. Preserve the exact fractions and do not replace them with approximate decimals unless preserving source-history display.

### 31.72 Generational LCM state

The File_15 convergence state `LCM(40, 70, 100) = 1400`. The value belongs to the authorized generational-unit triad and must not be treated as an ordinary span-family value without naming the active state.

### 31.73 Ordered endpoint-pair display

A bracket display in which the first member of one side pairs with the first member of the other side, and the second member pairs with the second member. File_15's `1406–1399 BC → 6 BC–AD 2` is the controlling example: `1406 BC → 6 BC` and `1399 BC → AD 2` are active. The display does not imply that every internal cross-pair has the same value.

### 31.74 File_15 restored cumulative Cainan dependency state

The File_15 state preserving `14466 BC → 6 BC = 14460` as a same-side arithmetic fact while leaving the restored cumulative Cainan mechanism dependency-controlled. Do not collapse this state with regular restored 2nd Cainan `+130`.

---

### 31.75 Raw data source state

A file-state in which the file supplies base chronological rows, tables, and lookup values for other rule-source files. `File_18` is the current example. A raw data source may preserve dependency-controlled operators without locally re-proving every operator it tabulates.

### 31.76 Minimum baseline table state

A table-state in which displayed rows give the minimum baseline values and variant values are derived only by named operators such as `+60 Terah`, `+215 Sojourn`, `+275`, restored regular 2nd Cainan `+130`, or restored cumulative 2nd Cainan `+460`. Do not treat a derived variant as a correction of the baseline.

### 31.77 Source-table state

A state in which the values in a table are governed by the active source tradition and table method. A source-table state may differ from another table in the same file because the method, witness tradition, or correction rule differs. Do not harmonize source-table values unless the local file explicitly opens that harmonization.

### 31.78 Shared year-label state

A year label occupied by multiple node-classes or traditions. Identity requires state, node-class, tradition, and operator agreement. `1876 BC` is the main repository-wide example, but other labels may function similarly.

### 31.79 Envelope member-label

A displayed member inside a range, week, or envelope. The member-label may be upper, lower, terminal, interior, Tishri, Nisan, or phase-resolved. It must not be treated as a correction of the whole envelope unless the local file states that it replaces the envelope.

### 31.80 Phase-resolved envelope member

An envelope member whose identity depends on an explicit Nisan / Tishri phase-state. Example: File_18 resolves the MT cumulative Peleg member `3495t` as the Tishri terminal member of the parallel Peleg span `3502t–3495t BC`, paired with the Nisan span `3501n–3494n BC`.

### 31.81 Website-to-minimum adjustment state

A source-history conversion state in which older website tables are translated back to repository minimum baselines by subtracting active website variants such as `+215 Sojourn`, `+60 Terah`, or `+275`. Preserve this as a publication-source adjustment, not as a new chronology.

### 31.82 SP Shem Superposition

The Samaritan Pentateuch Shem state in which Shem legitimately occupies both Noah-bound and Arphaxad-bound toggle positions. File_18 expresses this through the `599+ / 600` Noah-year issue and the `2990/2991 BC` / `2390/2391 BC` Shem pair. This state may ripple backward toward Adam but must not propagate forward past the Arphaxad-bound post-Flood row unless a local file explicitly opens that effect.

### 31.83 Abraham / Terah four-position field

The File_18 variant field in which Abraham has two birth states, `1951/2166 BC`, while Terah has four birth/death states because the `+60 Terah` state doubles the `±215 Sojourn` field. The four Terah states are `2021–1816 BC`, `2081–1876 BC`, `2236–2031 BC`, and `2296–2091 BC`. Terah's begetting display is `70/130 state-dependent`. This field remains four-position through 2nd Cainan if restored 2nd Cainan is inserted; the eight-position field begins at Arphaxad / Flood and propagates back to Adam.

### 31.84 2nd Cainan variant-boundary state

The File_18 state in which restored 2nd Cainan is inserted between Arphaxad and Shelah without making 2nd Cainan himself an eight-position row. 2nd Cainan retains four possible dates, like Terah: births `2611/2551 BC` and `2396/2336 BC`; deaths `2151/2091 BC` and `1936/1876 BC`. The eight-position field begins with Arphaxad / Flood and propagates back to Adam only when restored 2nd Cainan is explicitly opened. The resulting generational variant-count ladder is `12–8–4–2–0`: Adam through Arphaxad / Flood have eight possible dates; 2nd Cainan through Terah have four; Abraham through Joseph / Levi have two; Kohath and Amram have zero direct regular pivot dates; Aaron / Moses onward remain fixed / unaffected.

### 31.84a Pentateuchal Funnel / all-even structural harmony state

The File_18 structural state in which the restored-2nd-Cainan variant-count ladder maps onto the four primary Pentateuchal narrative arcs. When the full restored-Cainan regular chronology field is explicitly opened, Creation → Flood corresponds to `12` generations and `8` possible date states; Flood → Abraham corresponds to `8` generations and `4` possible date states; Abraham → entering Egypt corresponds to `4` generations and `2` possible date states; Egypt → Conquest / death of Moses corresponds to `2` floating generations and `0` direct regular pivot dates.

Claim-status note:
The generation counts and variant counts are arithmetic / table-structure facts once the active restored-Cainan state is opened. The mapping to the Pentateuchal narrative arcs is structural inference and theological / providential synchronization. It is not a new operator and must not alter table rows.


### 31.84b Cainan macro-vector B6 state

A File_16 / File_18 dependency state in which the MT Cumulative + 2nd Cainan table-source values supply macro-vector coordinates for the `1876 BC` Nexus. `File_18` supplies Cainan `4858–4851 BC`, Shelah / convergence envelope `4398–4391 BC`, selected `4396 BC`, regular Cainan `2551–2091 BC` and `2336–1876 BC`, and lower-bound Entry `1661 BC`. `File_16` controls the Category B / B6 classification through `2300`, `2520`, `2730`, and `1260 → 1290 → 1260`. This state does not add Category A event-node witnesses and does not replace the regular `+130` / cumulative `+460` Cainan distinction.

### 31.84c Audit-controlled `2307` / solar companion state

A companion audit state associated with the Cainan macro-vector. The same-side checks `4858 − 2551 = 2307` and `4398 − 2091 = 2307` may be retained as exploratory sanctuary / Hanukkah-style material. The solar comparison remains audit-controlled because `4398 − 1661 = 2737`, while `7.5 × 365 = 2737.5` before a phase-resolved calendar dependency is opened. Do not promote either companion into an active operator unless a later sanctuary / Hanukkah / phase-resolved solar file explicitly does so.

### 31.85 File_18 LXX corrected source-table state

The File_18 main LXX component-wise reconstruction uses Lamech `182/753` in both Regular and Cumulative execution; `571` is calculated and no surviving joint attestation is identified in the reviewed sources. Nahor retains `208`. LXX `777` is appendix-only; transmitted `188` is non-operative, with a qualified Key-of-23 hypothesis at File_18 §4A. Current hierarchy: §D.43.

### 31.86 MT cumulative Peleg phase-resolved Mirror application

The File_18 application of File_09 phase-resolved Mirror rules to MT cumulative Peleg. The Nisan span is `3501n–3494n BC`; the parallel Tishri span is `3502t–3495t BC`. The `3495t` member is a Tishri terminal member, not an error in the envelope.

### 31.87 SKL Pillars +30 Apparent Mode target

A dependency-controlled SKL display-state in which Pillars of Time rows become executable only when the active `+30 Apparent Mode` rail is named. Do not treat the Pillars row as ordinary whole-year arithmetic outside its rail state.

## D.3 Anchor-Tier Convention Note (registered June 10 2026)

Style Guide v2.5 applies the date label `anchor` functionally — "only when the date functions as an anchoring node" — and distinguishes `anchor`, `endpoint`, `boundary`, `block-member`, and `mirror target` as separate roles. Two tiers follow:

1. Repository endpoint anchors: terminal nodes from which chronological chains hang (for example Creation, Flood, Exodus, birth of Christ). These populate the Restart Capsule's Primary anchors inventory where capsule-governed.
2. Derived pegged dates / local anchoring nodes: dates fixed by deduction from a higher node, which then anchor further dates within their carrier files but are not repository endpoint anchors.

Registered instance (File_47 context): `1919 BC` (Levi birth, deduced as three years after Jacob's marriage in `1922 BC`) anchors `1782 BC` (Levi death at `137`) and the `1922–1915 BC` pregnancy / purification window; `1915 BC` (Joseph birth, after `1929 BC + 7 + 7`) anchors `1805 BC` (Joseph death at `110`) and the Serug diagonal lock `2065 − 1805 = 260`; cumulative-route Levi is `1936 BC` (band `1936/1933 BC`). These five are derived pegged dates carried by File_03 (Joseph), File_04 (Levi death), File_51a / File_20 (Levi birth), and File_18 / File_51a (cumulative `1936 BC`). They are correctly absent from the Restart Capsule Primary anchors inventory; File_47 declares no new anchors.

Machine Guard [ANCHOR TIER]:
Do not promote a derived pegged date to the Restart Capsule Primary anchors inventory merely because it functions as a local anchoring node, and do not strip the `anchor` label from a derived pegged date merely because it is not an endpoint. Name the tier when ambiguity matters.

### D.8.1 File_48 statistical-candor control cluster

The §2.0 control cluster governing every probability statement in File_48:
- Floor rule: a probability of the form `k/n` is an exact-enumeration rank — the observed configuration scored at
  least as high as `k` of the `n` decadal translations, including itself. `1/n` means unique global maximum, the
  resolution limit of the test, not a free-standing chance estimate.
- Conditionality: every File_48 probability is conditional on the stated target list, cycle set, rail configuration,
  list range, hub, and null convention, all chosen with the data visible.
- Documented sensitivity: the null-convention choice (Q-shift vs rigid) moves results roughly 2×; the published §2.2
  configuration moves from rank 4 of 31500 to rank 1033 of 441000 (`1 in 7875` to `1 in 427`) across documented
  alternatives.
- Documented multiplicity: across the 12 recorded §2.4 combinations, exact max-statistic correction moves the best
  nominal `1 in 2208` to `1 in 675` — a ceiling on the documented dimensions only, not a floor.
- Target-curation disclosure: documented in-sample forks (Methuselah dual node; Isaac excluded; 17→23 expansion;
  begetting ages only when divisible by 10; three-node expansion defined after the P13 observation) cannot be priced
  in; accordingly File_48 claims no unconditional probability anywhere.

Claim-status: methodological control state. Scope: File_48 only. Do not generalize the cluster to other files unless
a controlling dependency explicitly broadens it.

### D.8.2 File_48 Formation-Wobble dependency (convergence with §31.138)

File_48 §3.10 carries the 23-Wobble as a finding controlled by Final File_32, not locally derived. The values
converge with §31.138 (P23 `+50.669%` over n = `10`–`30`; peak `+153.333%` at n = `10`; baseline by n ≈ `48`).
File_48 adds an early-window reading — enrichment concentrated across the P1–P12 band where File_48's cumulative
sums are built, peaking near n = `9`–`10`, decaying to baseline by n ≈ `48` — carried as structural inference
dependent on the File_32 metric, not as a new state. The former File_48 §4.4.2 adjacent-gap phrasing was a wording
error (adjacent-gap divisibility by 23 sits at baseline) and is corrected to the pairwise k-difference metric; it is
not a competing measurement.

Machine Guard [FILE_48 WOBBLE DEPENDENCY]:
File_48 is not a second authority for Formation Wobble values. Cite Final File_32 (and §31.138) for any wobble
figure. File_48's early-window/decay reading is a File_32-dependent structural inference; do not promote it to a
standalone statistical state, and do not treat the corrected §4.4.2 adjacent-gap note as an alternative metric.

### D.8.3 File_48 membership-observation state

The structural-inference observation that the quadruplets the chronology projects into the Genesis-derived range
(`14466 BC` down to `1406 BC`) are the same low-ordinal set that carries the 23-enrichment: regular `Q − 5`
projection P2–P13; cumulative-sum projection P2–P10. This is an observation about shared membership of the early
quadruplets, not a claim that the date-spans between them are divisible by 23 (they generally are not; e.g.
`1866 → 12996 BC = 11130`).

Claim-status: structural inference; not load-bearing. Do not export this as a date-span divisibility claim or as
evidence of authorial design; it is a membership coincidence at the start of the sequence, left for the reader.

### D.9.3 File_53 claim hierarchy and burden rule

File_53 uses the following interpretive hierarchy:

| Level | Function |
|---|---|
| Textually explicit | Directly stated in Jubilees |
| Likely authorial | Strongly implied by BJ's structure and ideology |
| Plausibly authorial | Fits BJ's calendrical / genealogical grammar but is not explicit |
| Providential / over-intended | Coherent beyond likely authorial awareness |
| Mirror corroboration | Secondary stress-test only; never primary proof |

Claim-burden rule: the primary proof-burden remains the BJ internal architecture of 22 works / 22 heads, Cainan retained historically but expunged covenantally, the 13th solar-position logic, and the Fall-to-rest trunk. Mirror calculations, Key-of-23 expansions, cumulative MT/SP/LXX synchronizations, and Christological typology may corroborate or explain the structure, but they do not carry the primary proof-burden for likely BJ authorial design.

### D.9.4 File_53 Machine Guards

Eight Machine Guard entries are present in the pressure-tested File_53 source. The local `BJ CREATION-WEEK DUAL STATE` guard restates the file-level BJ Creation-week dual-state guard at the section where the state becomes active; it is counted separately because it appears as a separate Machine Guard block in File_53.

Machine Guard [FILE_53 CAINAN LEDGER NON-COLLAPSE]:
Cainan retained and Cainan expunged are simultaneous, non-competing ledgers. The historical / narrative ledger preserves Cainan; the theological / covenantal ledger omits Cainan from the sacred 22-count. Do not collapse the retained state into the expunged state or the expunged state into a denial of Cainan's historical function.

Machine Guard [FILE_53 COMPRESSED LEDGER NON-SUBSTITUTION]:
The Cainan-expunged `2400` field is a secondary compressed ledger. It does not replace the primary BJ Fall-to-rest trunk `3849 BC → 1399 BC = 2450`, nor the `File_51c` Creation-to-Conquest trunk `3856 BC → 1406 BC = 2450`.

Machine Guard [FILE_53 BJ CREATION-WEEK DUAL STATE]:
Do not flatten the two BJ Creation-week models into one state. The endpoint model is `3863–3856 BC`; the Adam-Day model is `3861–3854 BC`; the Adam-Day `+30` overlay is `3891–3884 BC` with single anchor `3886 BC`.

Machine Guard [FILE_53 MIRROR HIERARCHY]:
Mirror calculations in Part III are secondary stress-tests. They may corroborate a pattern already grounded in BJ's textual and structural field, but they must not be used as the primary proof of BJ authorial intent.

Machine Guard [FILE_53 CROSS-AXIS OPERATOR]:
Where a BC/AD cross-axis span is measured as a civil span, use `BC + AD − 1`. A-space / Protocol 1 Mirror labels must remain distinct from same-side BC subtraction and from ordinary civil cross-axis measurement.

Machine Guard [FILE_53 49/50 NON-COLLAPSE]:
BJ literal jubilees remain 49 years. Macro-50 structure may be opened where the file explicitly invokes `2450 = 50 × 49` or `2500 = 50 × 50`, but the macro-50 register does not erase the literal 49-register.

Machine Guard [BJ CREATION-WEEK DUAL STATE]:
Do not flatten these two BJ Creation-week models into one state.

Machine Guard [FILE_53 MIRROR SECONDARY SUPPORT]:
Do not treat Part III as the proof-burden carrier for the file. The primary proof-burden remains the BJ internal architecture of 22 works / 22 heads, Cainan retained historically but expunged covenantally, and the Fall-to-rest trunk.

### D.10.2 File_50c operator and scan-state controls

File_50c uses the File_50a input values:

$$P_{nat}=P-11.$$

$$V_{90}=round(P_{nat}/90)\times90.$$

The Bipartite Grid Landing operator is:

$$G=-V_{90}.$$

$$M=round(G/720)\times720.$$

$$R=G-M.$$

| Condition | Signed target-space output | Civil-coordinate conversion |
|---|---|---|
| `R ∈ [−360,0)` | `{M, M − 720}` | `Civil BC = \|Target\| + 6` |
| `R ∈ (0,+360]` | `{M, M + 720}` | `Civil BC = \|Target\| + 6` |
| `R = 0` | `{M, M − 720, M + 720}` | `Civil BC = \|Target\| + 6` |

Executable exact-strike inventory after File_50c pressure testing: P2, P6, P13, P17, P19, P23, P56, P73, P78, P83, P91, and P98. P1 is source-history only in the inherited exact-strike list because P1 is bypass-controlled. P83 is executable because `389520 = 541 × 720`.

Corrected File_50c scan state:

| Field | Corrected value |
|---|---:|
| Silent indices | `72` |
| Hitting indices | `28` |
| Structural target families | `16` |
| Boundary concentration | `22 → 11` |
| Intermediate concentration | `6 → 5` |

Half-step tie convention note: when `V_90 = 720q + 360`, two signed `M` values are equally near. File_50c preserves exact integer arithmetic and an invariant unordered civil bracket in all such cases. An ordered `base` / `companion` display requires a documented tie convention if a downstream implementation needs ordered signed-`M` labels.

`Dual polarity` and signed target-space in this file describe the Bipartite `±720` landing around `M`. They are not File_32 / File_33 raw prime-space cross-polarity, positive-polarity `Q − 5 / Q + 25`, negative-polarity / signed A-space `Q − 16 / Q + 14`, or Coordinate Mirror / Protocol 1.

Machine Guard [FILE_50C P23 NATURALIZED GRID]:
P23 `31686 BC` is the File_50a Naturalized Grid civil landing. Do not collapse it with P23 positive-polarity `31716/31746 BC`, negative-polarity AD `31705/31735`, raw prime-space cross-polarity, or secondary Coordinate Mirror displays.

Machine Guard [FILE_50C +2 / +720 FIREWALL]:
The SKL `+2 local rail` is a two-year local coordinate shift. The `+720 scale-state` is the cosmic `720 = 2 × 360` interval used by the Bipartite bracket. They are related scale states but are not interchangeable operators.

Machine Guard [FILE_50C GENERATED BRACKET / LIST HIT]:
The Bipartite operator generates a two-position signed bracket, or an exact-strike signed tri-position. `Single` and `Dual` describe one or two admitted civil-coordinate intersections, not generation multiplicity. The scan admits primary and source-parenthetical coordinates in ancient-list rows, excludes the contextual `536 BC` / `486 BC` anchor rows and File_50c §5 meta-anchors, and compresses hits by coordinate-family row identity. Coordinate-identical duplicate rows do not create additional targets.

Machine Guard [FILE_50C RESTORATION / COMPUTATIONAL ANCHORS]:
`536 BC` is the biblical restoration-anchor state. `486 BC` is the Berossus computational-anchor state. They are not an uncertainty pair, one slash-pair node, or interchangeable historical anchors.

Machine Guard [FILE_50C TEXTUAL TARGET / COMPUTED COORDINATE]:
SKL and Berossus names and list ordering are source-retained textual or reception-history data. The BC coordinates in File_50c Appendix A are repository calculations or dependency-controlled target states. Do not present the computed coordinates as direct tablet wording.

### D.11.2 Moses-node operator and File_23 Cainan boundary

The File_55 primer uses the fixed nodes:

```markdown
1486 BC; 1446 BC; 1406 BC
```

For integer input date `D`, select the unique node `N` for which:

$$
D-N\equiv0\pmod 3.
$$

Define:

$$
T=\frac{D-N}{3},
$$

$$
M=D-T=N+2T,
$$

$$
R=M-N=2T.
$$

The ordinary display is:

```markdown
D → M → N
```

For an input later than its selected node, retain the signed result; do not force a positive span. If two endpoints route to the same node, the marker-to-marker lifespan is exactly two-thirds of the source lifespan. A uniform same-node shift contracts by two-thirds, so `+60 → +40`. If an input shift also changes the selected node, use the full operator rather than a same-node shortcut.

State guard — File_23 Cainan `460` / schematic `480`:
File_23 does not assign Cainan a `480`-year lifespan. Cainan remains `460`. In File_23, `480` belongs to the rounded Shem-to-Eber + Cainan `4+1` average-class and to the schematic `480`-year displacement between the with-/without-Cainan halving-table heads. Neither display replaces File_55's cumulative restored-Cainan operator `+460`.

Anchor-tier note:
The File_55 year-labels `175206 BC`, `175686 BC`, `179526 BC`, `180006 BC`, `23526 BC`, `184326 BC`, `311526 BC`, `311046 BC`, `18726 BC`, `14406 BC`, `10126 BC`, `10146 BC`, `9806 BC`, `9826 BC`, `8886 BC`, `8606 BC`, `4026 BC`, `3706 BC`, `3206 BC`, and `806 BC` are derived third markers or local anchoring nodes under Register §D.3. They are not automatically Repository endpoint anchors and do not require promotion to the Restart Capsule Primary anchors inventory merely because they anchor later File_55 comparisons. External inputs such as `268566 / 269286 / 466566 / 465846 / 275766 / 39486 / 3606 BC` retain their File_21 / File_24 / File_34 node-classes and are not redefined by File_55.

### D.11.3 File_55 Machine Guards

Machine Guard [REGULAR `+130` / CUMULATIVE `+460`]:
Restored 2nd Cainan contributes `130` in Regular begetting chronology and `460` in cumulative lifespan chronology. Do not substitute one value for the other.

Machine Guard [LIFESPAN VS. CHRONOLOGY FIREWALL]:
Cumulative lifespan dates are biographical volumes stacked end-to-end. They are not ordinary historical birth dates, and they must not be conflated with sequential narrative spans such as the `430`-year sojourn.

Machine Guard [APPARENT-AGE LOCALIZATION]:
The `+30 Apparent Age` is a localized Adamic overlay. It does not propagate through later patriarchal dates unless a local schematic state explicitly defines such propagation.

Machine Guard [THIRD MARKER NODE-CLASS]:
A third marker is a derived coordinate. It may coincide with a historical, schematic, Rounded, Actual, prophetic, or typological node, but identity requires agreement of state, node-class, and operator.

Machine Guard [SHARED YEAR-LABEL]:
A year label shared by multiple states is not automatically the same node. `3706 BC`, `4106 BC`, `4116 BC`, and other repeated labels must retain their source state and operator.

Machine Guard [FLOOD / SHEM-DEATH / CAINAN NODE-CLASS]:
`4836 BC` is the base Rounded cumulative Flood / Shem-death / Arphaxad boundary; `5296 BC` is its restored-Cainan `+460` counterpart; `4856 BC` is restored cumulative Cainan's birth / start; and `4396 BC` is Cainan's death / Shelah boundary. The cumulative `+460` shifts Adam through the Arphaxad / Flood / Shem-death boundary. It does not propagate beyond the local Cainan life `4856–4396 BC`; Shelah and all later cumulative coordinates remain unchanged.

The `14406 BC` harmonic is `10×` only in its span to `6 BC`: `14400 = 10 × 1440`; it is not the decimal multiplication of the year-label `1446 BC`.

### D.11.4 Methodological / null-test boundary

Source-control note:
The arithmetic results in this file do not by themselves establish that the Moses-node primer detects intentional chronological design. The selector always produces an output; the recognized-number vocabulary is dense; many rows use Rounded chronology or admitted state choices; and several recurring effects—including unique routing, `+60 → +40`, and same-node two-thirds contraction—are algebraic consequences of the operator.

The source working record identifies the LXX Shem-death `1290 → 430` row and Abraham’s call `2091 → 1876 → 1446` as the two lowest-slack examples. Even these remain selected instances rather than evidence until tested against a null chronology.

In the external sample, one exact `mod2160` member within a qualifying `±720` bracket is algebraically expected. The evidentiary objects are therefore the marker landings, endpoint harmonics, and retained misses. Ubara-Tutu / Noah remains explicitly retained as a miss in the current sample.

The required test is to predefine the counted operations, target-number vocabulary, admitted variants, and hit criterion; sweep all real inputs while retaining misses; and compare the resulting hit rate with the identical procedure applied to shuffled and random chronologies. Unless the observed rate exceeds the null rate, the hypothesis remains unestablished and is not asserted. If the test does not separate the chronology from chance, the line of inquiry is dropped; if it does, the two lowest-slack cases are the appropriate starting point for a stronger argument.

#### D.12.2 File_52a Machine Guards

When an executable BC-to-AD civil span is opened, use `BC + AD − 1 = total`. Do not use ordinary signed subtraction across the epoch boundary unless an explicitly labeled A-space calculation is being performed.

A-space may be used to display mirror coordinates or signed cross-axis relations. A-space display does not replace civil BC/AD labels in ordinary chronological statements.

When a rounded span is inverted and the raw inverse falls off the mod-5 lattice, normalize to the nearest mod-5 value before reapplying the span chronologically.

The inverse architecture is trunk-first. Do not promote branch coincidences above the regular trunk `2700 → 7200`, the cumulative trunk `12600 → 62100`, or the `9900` transfer bridge.

### D.21.3 File_57 Machine Guards

Machine Guard [SELECTED LISTS ONLY]:
Do not infer that every biblical list is a chronology cipher. File_57 applies only to bounded lists satisfying its selection criteria.

Machine Guard [LEDGER STATE NON-COLLAPSE]:
Personnel totals, printed-number totals, key-withheld totals, and reconciled totals are distinct states. Do not collapse `217100`, `217122`, `220800`, and `220822`.

Machine Guard [JEHOIADA INCLUSION UNRESOLVED]:
Do not silently decide whether Jehoiada's `3700` is included in or additional to Levi's `4600`.

Machine Guard [ZADOK KEY NON-ADDITION]:
Zadok plus `22` primarily defines operative `23`; ordinary personnel addition is secondary and must be labeled.

Machine Guard [OPERATOR PREDECLARATION]:
Declare list boundary, ledger, anchor, direction, scale, and operator before calculation.

Machine Guard [SOURCE ORDER / CATEGORY ORDER NON-COLLAPSE]:
Preserve source order and declared category order as simultaneous states; do not present a regrouped sequence as the printed order.

Machine Guard [DISPLAYED NUMERAL SUFFICIENCY]:
Count only supplied numerals in a symbolic printed-number ledger; do not guess unnumbered quantities.

Machine Guard [CATEGORY ORDER LOCAL PROOF]:
The method is general in hypothesis but local in proof; every list must independently declare boundary, categories, sort keys, coverage, operator, and null tests.

Machine Guard [FILE_57 / FILE_58 CORPUS ROUTING]:
File_57 controls the general method and compact precedent; File_58 controls the full Numbers 28–29 and Genesis application corpus.

Machine Guard [AUTHORIAL / PROVIDENTIAL FIREWALL]:
Remote alignments do not prove ancient authorial knowledge; use historical-critical or providential classification as warranted.

Machine Guard [18200 NODE-CLASS NON-COLLAPSE]:
File_57's list-derived `18200` and File_44's rounded/display-only `18200` motif share arithmetic but not node-class or operator.

Machine Guard [240486 SHARED-LABEL NON-COLLAPSE]:
File_57's derived `240486 BC` and File_21's Alalngar maximum-state coordinate remain distinct states.

Machine Guard [FILE MAP RESTRAINT]:
The File map is a compact retrieval aid, not a duplicate prose summary.

Machine Guard [NO PARTIAL-HIT PROOF]:
A proposed list cipher must account for awkward and non-obvious figures; one attractive subtotal is insufficient.

Machine Guard [KEY-OF-23 WHOLE-NUMBER STATE]:
File_57's `25/23` calculations are ordinary exact transforms; do not import Residue or Mirror protocols without declaration.

Machine Guard [ISSACHARITE COTERIE CLAIM RESTRAINT]:
An Issachar-associated specialist circle is a historical-critical hypothesis, not a textual datum or proven institution.

Machine Guard [ISSACHAR GRAND-TOTAL NON-COLLAPSE]:
`87000` is the public encompassing personnel total; `145600` is an overlapping printed-number ledger only.

Machine Guard [REMOTE PROJECTION NON-SUBSTITUTION]:
Deep-time projections corroborate but do not replace local list arithmetic or historical chronology.

Machine Guard [MESSIANIC ENDPOINT RESTRAINT]:
The triple-`480` rail is arithmetic fact; deliberate Chronicler prediction of `6 BC` is not established.

Machine Guard [SKL / BEROSSUS NON-IDENTITY]:
Arithmetic comparison does not collapse biblical, SKL, and Berossus source states or prove direct literary dependence.

Machine Guard [QUARTER-CONTRACTION DECLARATION]:
The `60000` backward arm is a declared `÷4` comparison state, not the unmodified western total.

Machine Guard [BIDIRECTIONAL DISPLAY NON-MIRROR]:
The `666 × 360` display is a bidirectional count-to-chronology comparison, not automatically Protocol 1, Protocol 2, inverse architecture, or formal Mirror.

Modal-state rule:
A shared number, date-label, animal, species, sex, category, or sequence does not create identity unless list boundary, ledger state, category partition, sequence operator, node-class, unit, and chronology operator agree.

### D.22.3 File_58 Machine Guards

Machine Guard [FILE MAP RESTRAINT]:
The File map exists for AI retrieval. Do not expand it into a prose-heavy duplicate of the heading hierarchy.

Machine Guard [SELECTED LISTS ONLY]:
The authorial methodology permits testing potentially broad narrative-list application, including Genesis lists, but no list becomes an active cipher merely because it contains numbers. Each list must satisfy the File_57 selection and anti-cherry-picking controls.

Machine Guard [DISPLAYED-NUMERAL SUFFICIENCY]:
In a declared symbolic list state, count the numerals supplied by the text. Unnumbered dependents, offspring, attendants, or implied members do not add an inferred quantity to the cipher ledger. The surface narrative remains valid, but speculative reconstruction of an unstated physical total is outside the printed-number operator.

Machine Guard [ALL ROWS USED ONCE]:
A declared category-order sequence must use every bounded numerical row exactly once unless the file explicitly opens an overlapping printed-number state. Do not omit an inconvenient row, duplicate a favorable row, or invent an unstated quantity.

Machine Guard [CATEGORY BASIS REQUIRED]:
Reordering must follow a category supplied by the text or controlled by the canonical corpus: species, sex, clean/unclean classification, good/bad polarity, maternal household, festival unit, or another explicitly justified category. A chronology target may not generate the category after the fact.

Machine Guard [SEQUENCE PREDECLARATION]:
Declare the primary and secondary sort keys before calculating chronology. State whether the active sequence is source order, sex-major order, purity order, polarity order, maternal-household order, frame order, or local reversal.

Machine Guard [MINIMAL DETERMINISTIC REORDERING]:
Once the category hierarchy is declared, preserve the original relative order inside each category unless a second sort key is also declared. Prefer the smallest deterministic transformation that accounts for all rows.

Machine Guard [SOURCE ORDER / CIPHER ORDER NON-COLLAPSE]:
The printed narrative sequence and a declared cipher sequence are simultaneous states. Do not rewrite the biblical text as though the category-major order were its surface order.

Machine Guard [CLEAN / UNCLEAN CLAIM SPLIT]:
Species and sex are textual data. Clean / unclean classification is a canonical Torah classification. The chronological function of the partition is structural inference.

Machine Guard [ESAU 550 NON-HEADCOUNT SPECULATION]:
The Genesis 32 symbolic total is the sum of the displayed numerals, `550`. The words “with their young” do not license guessing an additional camel-young count. The physical herd may have contained unnumbered young, but that question is outside the cipher ledger.

Machine Guard [FIVE-DROVE SOURCE ORDER]:
Preserve Genesis 32’s five textual droves—goats, sheep, camels, cattle, and donkeys—as a source-order state. Category-major sequences may be tested separately, but they must not replace the printed procession.

Machine Guard [SEX / PURITY DUAL-PARTITION]:
The displayed numerals yield `490/60` under both purity and sex partitions. State which partition is active. Do not treat the two partitions as identical categories merely because their totals coincide.

Machine Guard [FEMALE 400/430/470/490 VALUE REGISTER]:
The female source-order cumulative values `400`, `430`, `470`, and `490` may be compared with the Affliction–Exodus, Entry–Exodus, Entry–Conquest, and LXX Haran–Conquest spans. They do not form one single-anchor chronology walk; each comparison must name its own endpoints.

Machine Guard [DONKEY ROW TRANSLATION STATE]:
The final donkey row may be rendered “male donkeys” or “young donkeys / foals” in English translations. File_58 may use the male-row state only where the chosen textual rendering is declared. The printed numeral `10` remains stable.

Machine Guard [MALE-FIRST CLEAN SEQUENCE]:
For the active clean chronology sequence, use male goats `20`, rams `20`, bulls `10`, female goats `200`, ewes `200`, and cows `40`. This is the declared sex-major sequence, not the Genesis source order.

Machine Guard [UNCLEAN FRAMING SEQUENCE]:
For the active unclean chronology sequence, use female donkeys `20`, camels `30`, and male donkeys `10`. This is the declared donkey-frame sequence, not the Genesis source order.

Machine Guard [GENESIS 33 / GENESIS 46 CROSS-SCENE ORDER]:
The `23 + 33 + 14` order is not the printed Genesis 46 order. It is a declared cross-scene sequence supplied by Genesis 33: handmaids and children, Leah and children, Rachel and Joseph. Preserve both source orders as distinct states.

Machine Guard [HANDMAID 23 / KEY-OF-23 NON-AUTOMATICITY]:
The handmaid subtotal `16 + 7 = 23` is an arithmetic list value. It may be compared with the repository Key of 23, but it does not automatically activate the `25/23` operator or any Key-of-23 expansion without a separately declared state.

Machine Guard [LOCAL 460 NON-COLLAPSE]:
The span `1906 BC → 1446 BC = 460` belongs to the Benjamin-to-Exodus comparison generated by the `23/33/14` sequence. Do not collapse it with the regular or cumulative 2nd Cainan `460`, Watcher `460`, or another same-valued repository state.

Machine Guard [BENJAMIN 1906 SHARED-LABEL]:
The `1906 BC` landing in the `23/33/14` sequence may be compared with File_01’s Benjamin birth label. Do not collapse it with the BJ Abraham Call state also labeled `1906 BC`.

Machine Guard [1873 FAMINE COORDINATE]:
`1873 BC` is a derived late-famine comparison point, two years before the `1871 BC` famine-end shorthand. Do not promote it to a primary event anchor without separate source control.

Machine Guard [GENESIS 46 SUBTOTAL / HEADCOUNT NON-COLLAPSE]:
The four printed household subtotals `33`, `16`, `14`, and `7` sum to `70`, while Genesis 46 separately states `66` direct descendants who came with Jacob and `70` for the house of Jacob in Egypt. Preserve the printed-subtotal, entrant, and household-total ledgers as distinct textual states.

Machine Guard [GENESIS 46 VERSE-TOTAL CONTROL]:
File_58 uses the explicit verse-level totals `33`, `16`, `14`, and `7`. Do not silently repair the Leah-name enumeration, add unnamed persons, or subtract named persons in order to manufacture the stated `33`.

Machine Guard [MT 70 / LXX 75 NON-COLLAPSE]:
The MT household total `70` and the LXX / Acts comparison total `75` are distinct textual-tradition states. File_58’s present maternal-household arithmetic is MT `70`; the `75` state must be separately declared before use.

Machine Guard [SEVENTY PERSONS / SEVENTY YEARS CLAIM SPLIT]:
The household total `70` is textual datum. The projection `1876 BC → 1806 BC = 70` is structural inference under count-to-chronology. Do not state that Genesis 46 explicitly converts the persons into years.

Machine Guard [FAMINE-CONTEXT PRECEDENT]:
Genesis 41 explicitly equates the seven good and seven bad cows with years, and Genesis 45 places Jacob’s household descent within that same seven-year famine field. Treat this as proximate narrative warrant for testing the household `70` as years, not as an explicit equation of persons with years.

Machine Guard [FLOCK TYPOLOGY CLAIM STATUS]:
The description of Jacob’s seventy-person covenant household as God’s flock is a typological / theological reading. It may explain why a people-count could participate in the animal-time grammar, but it does not carry the arithmetic proof burden.

Machine Guard [ENOCH FLOCK / YEAR NON-COLLAPSE]:
1 Enoch 89 establishes the symbolic substitution of Jacob’s sons and descendants as sheep and flocks within a historical apocalypse. It does not state that their numerical total equals years. Use 1 Enoch as reception-history evidence for the flock encoding, Genesis 41 as the explicit animal-year precedent, and classify the application of Genesis 46’s `70` to a seventy-year span as structural inference.

Machine Guard [RACHEL 14 / JOSEPH 7+7 CLAIM SPLIT]:
Rachel’s household subtotal `14`, Joseph’s inclusion within that household, and Joseph’s interpretation of `7 + 7` cows as `7 + 7` years are textual data and arithmetic facts. The proposal that Rachel’s `14` intentionally encodes Joseph’s fourteen cow-years is structural inference, not textual equation.

Machine Guard [BILHAH 7 SINGLE-HEPTAD RESTRAINT]:
Bilhah’s household subtotal `7` is an explicit textual total. It may be compared with Rachel’s `14` as one heptad beside two heptads, but it must not be converted into years without a separately declared operator and target.

Machine Guard [RACHEL / LEAH LEXICAL RESTRAINT]:
Rachel’s name securely means “ewe.” Leah’s proposed “cow / wild cow” etymology is disputed and competes with a derivation related to weariness. Rachel’s lexical datum may support flock symbolism; Leah’s bovine proposal remains collateral only.

Machine Guard [1806 SHARED-LABEL NON-COLLAPSE]:
File_58’s `1806 BC` Entry-plus-70 coordinate is a derived comparison node. It must not be collapsed with File_03’s Rounded Scaffold clarity-only Joseph death at `1806 BC`, File_03’s BJ Abraham death at `1806 BC`, or Joseph’s standard death at `1805 BC`.

Machine Guard [JOSEPH ROUNDED 40/30/40 STATE]:
The sequence `1916 → 1876 → 1846 → 1806 = 40 + 30 + 40` is active only under the File_51a Rounded Scaffold comparison state. Preserve `1876 BC` as Entry and `1846 BC` as Affliction / Bondage origin. Do not convert the rounded endpoints into ordinary MT event dates or use the symmetry to overwrite File_03.

Machine Guard [ROUNDED JOSEPH AGE NON-LITERAL]:
The Rounded Scaffold span `1916 BC → 1876 BC = 40` is not Joseph’s textual age at Entry. The textual chronology remains `30 + 7 + 2 = 39`, with standard Joseph dates `1915–1805 BC`. The rounded `1916–1806 BC` life is methodological and comparison-only.

Machine Guard [JACOB MOURNING 40 / 70 CLAIM SPLIT]:
Genesis 50 explicitly states forty embalming days and seventy mourning days. The `30 + 40 = 70` reading is a derived remainder display, not an independently printed `30`, and it presumes the forty-day embalming interval is included within the seventy-day mourning horizon.

Machine Guard [JOSEPH POLARITY PRECEDENT]:
Genesis 41 explicitly equates cows with years and distinguishes good and bad groups. This supplies textual warrant for animal-year and polarity methods; it does not prove every later animal list without local controls.

Machine Guard [NUMBERS-ONLY / LEVITICUS-16 FIREWALL]:
The controlling `280` total is Numbers-only. The Leviticus 16 rite is a separate overlay state and must not be silently folded into the base ledger.

Machine Guard [LEVITICUS-16 OVERLAY LEDGER]:
The Leviticus 16 `+5` is the complete displayed ritual-animal ledger: `1 bull + 2 rams + 2 goats = 5`. It includes the live goat because the text numerically specifies two goats within the paired rite. The slaughtered-offering subset is `4` and must not be substituted silently for the displayed ritual ledger. Declare the overlay explicitly as `285` or `287` according to the active Sabbath state. No independent chronological significance is claimed for `285` or `287` in this file.

Machine Guard [PRIESTLY / PROPHETIC / ENOCHIAN NON-COLLAPSE]:
Priestly `336`, Prophetic `360`, and Enochian `364` are distinct registers. The fixed-week `280` state is Enochian `364`, even though the list itself is sacrificial and priestly in function.

Machine Guard [1260 / 1274 PARALLEL STATES]:
`1260 = 3.5 × 360` and `1274 = 3.5 × 364` are parallel calendar states. Do not describe one as a correction of the other without an explicitly declared transformation.

Machine Guard [FESTIVAL REVERSAL NON-MIRROR]:
A local festival-order reversal is not Protocol 1, Protocol 2, inverse-number reversal, or formal Mirror.

Machine Guard [MT / LXX / SP OFFSET LOCALITY]:
The `−33`, `−182`, and `−215` shifts are local comparison operators. Do not propagate them globally or collapse them with Sojourn `215`, Exodus `215`, or unrelated variant mechanisms.

Machine Guard [T/N PHASE DECLARATION]:
Every `t/n` landing must state its phase convention and be checked component-wise. An unsuffixed integer subtraction does not by itself prove a paired Tishri/Nisan label.

Machine Guard [KINGS 49/50 NON-CORRECTION]:
The historical and schematic Kings ledgers are adjacent states. The goats’ `49` may corroborate a `49` member but does not repair or replace the Kings chronology.

Machine Guard [REGULAR / CUMULATIVE NON-COLLAPSE]:
Cumulative chronology is a method, not a fourth manuscript tradition. Its dates and operators must remain dependency-controlled.

Machine Guard [JOHANNINE ENDPOINT DEPENDENCY]:
`AD 31t/32n` and `AD 32t` remain proposal states until their phase arithmetic and File_13 dependencies are verified.

Machine Guard [AUTHORIAL / PROVIDENTIAL FIREWALL]:
Local list construction may support a historical-critical design hypothesis. Remote chronological or cross-corpus agreement does not prove ancient access; classify it as providential synchronization or exploratory corroboration where appropriate.

Machine Guard [FILE_57 / FILE_58 ROUTING]:
File_57 retains the compact Numbers 28–29 precedent and routes the full sacrificial ledger, manuscript lanes, historical applications, narrative-list sequences, and Johannine synthesis to File_58. File_20 Recommended Cross-File Update 22 is fulfilled by File_58. Do not reverse this control direction.

Machine Guard [MIDRASH-LIKE NON-ANACHRONISM]:
The term `midrash-like` may describe associative numerical interpretation, intertextual regrouping, or list-sensitive reading that could be intelligible in an ancient scribal setting. It must not be used to claim that the source belongs to a later formal rabbinic genre or to prove compositional intent.

Machine Guard [GENESIS 46 APPENDIX SEQUENCE PREDECLARATION]:
A sequence must arise from the printed list, a textual cross-scene order, or a declared structural pairing before dates are tested. Do not permute the four subtotals merely to reach a desired chronology target.

- File_57 controls the general selected-list method and compact Numbers 28–29 precedent. File_58 controls the full ledger, Genesis applications, MT / LXX / SP lanes, reception-history evidence, and Johannine proposal.
- File_59 controls the downstream Numbers 1 / Numbers 26 military-census matrix. It may cite File_58's `182`, `1260/1274`, and `360/364` grammar by dependency without reopening File_58's sacrificial-ledger proof.
- File_20 Recommended Cross-File Update 22 is fulfilled by Final File_58. File_14 controls the normative `270/280` gestation axiom pair; File_20 controls BJ / Jubilees and the Enochian `273` companion within the non-collapse `270/273/280` triad.
- Use File_12 / File_17 for `336/360/364` and `1260/1274`; File_04 / File_16 for Jacob / Entry / Affliction / Exile state control; File_03 / File_51a for Joseph actual / rounded non-collapse; File_09 for phase arithmetic; File_13 for later Johannine dependency review.
- Appendix D Genesis 46 chronology tests, `1806 BC`, `1873 BC`, and Benjamin `1906 BC` are future-study comparison coordinates only; they are not Primary anchors.

### D.23.3 File_59 Machine Guards

Machine Guard [CENSUS MEAN DERIVED-STATE]:
`602640` is a derived reconciliation ledger generated from the two textual military census totals. It is not a third biblical census and must not be cited as though Numbers states it directly.

Machine Guard [LEVITE METRIC NON-COLLAPSE]:
The Levitical totals count males one month old and upward, whereas the military totals count fighting-age males twenty years old and upward. Do not add the two ledgers or compare them as homogeneous personnel totals without an explicitly declared mixed-metric comparison state.

Machine Guard [OFFICIAL LEVITE TOTAL SOURCE]:
Use the stated totals `22000` and `23000` for the official Levitical mean. Also preserve the transmitted Numbers 3 clan rows `7500 + 8600 + 6200 = 22300` as a distinct arithmetic subtotal. File_59 makes no judgment about scribal repair and does not silently replace either state with the other.

Machine Guard [MOD-100 FIRSTBORN DISPLAY CONTROL]:
Across the twenty-four military tribal totals in Numbers 1 and Numbers 26, `22/24 = 11/12` are divisible by `100`; the exceptions are first-census Gad `45650` and second-census Reuben `43730`. Therefore `22273 → 22300` is an admitted nearest-hundred census-format display. It does not replace exact `22273`, official `22000`, or the independently generated MT clan-row subtotal `22300`.

Machine Guard [FIRSTBORN 270/273/300 NON-COLLAPSE]:
`270`, `273`, and `300` are distinct states: mapped Prophetic gestation, exact Enochian / firstborn excess, and MT clan-subtotal / one-month-threshold completion. Do not substitute one value for another without declaring the operator.

Machine Guard [EXACT 273 INCLUSIVE CONTROL]:
The exact subtractions produce `727` and `2547`. Their Enochian closures `728` and `2548` require inclusive counting. Do not present the inclusive values as ordinary same-side subtraction.

Machine Guard [VARIANT-RAIL RECONCILIATION NON-COLLAPSE]:
File_04’s `+215 Position`, MT Minimum `−215`, and LXX `−33` family-unit states remain independent. The present subtotal parent rail belongs to the Sojourn-`215` comparison field; its `1440` translation and the LXX rail’s `1290` translation remain distinct even when both terminate at `606–536 BC`.

Machine Guard [ISAAC AGE-STATE RESTRAINT]:
Isaac’s age-two weaning is a conventional comparison state, not a number supplied by Genesis. Isaac age `15` at sacrifice is controlled by File_20’s BJ / Jubilees chronology and MT transfer, not by the Hebrew wording “lad” alone.

Machine Guard [REVELATION LEVI POSITION CONTROL]:
Levi is eighth in Revelation 7 after seven completed `12000` groups. Do not call Levi seventh, and do not describe Levi’s inclusion and Dan’s omission as a proven direct one-for-one replacement.

Machine Guard [LEVITICAL BC-SIDE TERMINUS]:
The proof-bearing Levitical extension terminates at the `606–536 BC` captivity-restoration field. Later AD, bilateral, or `630000 + 630000` continuations are omitted or appendix-only and cannot carry the local proof.

Machine Guard [SOURCE-ORDER PRESERVATION]:
Preserve the Numbers 1 and Numbers 26 transmitted tribe orders before applying any normalized, reverse, camp, or birth-order operator.

Machine Guard [EPHRAIM-MANASSEH ORDER NON-COLLAPSE]:
Numbers 1 and Numbers 26 differ in the Ephraim / Manasseh sequence. A normalized comparison order does not replace either source order.

Machine Guard [CATEGORY-ORDER PREDECLARATION]:
Marching-camp order, birth order, sex, office, or another category order may be used only when the category source and deterministic ordering rule are declared before chronology is tested.

Machine Guard [CAMP-ORDER DECLARATION]:
Quadrant totals require the Numbers 2 / Numbers 10 marching arrangement. Do not describe camp-order results as though Numbers 26 prints the tribes in that order.

Machine Guard [BIRTH-ORDER EXPLORATORY]:
The Reuben-to-Benjamin ledger remains appendix-only until the complete birth-order dataset, Joseph-son ordering alternatives, failures, and null-test conditions are reported.

Machine Guard [ANCHOR-FAMILY DECLARATION]:
`1446 BC`, `1466 BC`, `1476 BC`, `1406 BC`, and `6 BC` are distinct anchor states. Name the active anchor before projecting a census or Revelation value.

Machine Guard [CENSUS-ANCHOR HORIZON]:
Numbers 1:1 dates the first census to the second month of the second year after the Exodus. `1446 BC` is the repository's primary Exodus / census anchor horizon for the count-to-chronology matrix; do not silently convert that anchor convention into a claim that the month-dated census occurred within the same civil year under every calendrical reconstruction.

Machine Guard [LOCAL DERIVED COORDINATE NON-PROMOTION]:
`604086 BC` and the other File_59 outputs are local derived coordinates unless a later control-file update formally promotes them. Do not silently add them to the repository-wide Primary-anchor inventory.

Machine Guard [ROUNDED NON-RETROJECTION]:
Rounded Creation, Actual MT Creation, Year-6 / Adam, and rounded death states remain distinct. A shared output date does not authorize retrojection from one state into another.

Machine Guard [SKL STATE NON-COLLAPSE]:
SKL Short base, Short `+720`, Long base, and maximum states are simultaneous comparison rails, not corrections of one another. Name the active state before subtraction.

Machine Guard [BEROSSUS-SKL NODE-CLASS FIREWALL]:
Berossus Alorus / Adam, SKL Pre-Creation, SKL Adam / Alulim, and their Flood states remain distinct textual and node-class states even where a rigid translation relates their coordinates.

Machine Guard [PRECESSIONAL UNIT DECLARATION]:
`6480` years, `6480` precessional days, `90 × 72` years, and one quarter of a Great Year are related but not interchangeable labels. State the active unit and scale.

Machine Guard [CIVIL CROSS-AXIS YEAR-ZERO]:
Every BC-to-AD elapsed span uses `BC + AD − 1`. Do not use direct addition without the civil year-zero correction.

Machine Guard [DUAL-144000 PLACEMENT]:
`145446 BC` and `460086 BC` arise from different placements of the same `144000` block. Preserve the initial-placement and terminal-placement states separately.

Machine Guard [REVELATION MIXED-UNIT RESTRAINT]:
The `144000` persons and `1600` stadia are heterogeneous textual units. Their symbolic numeral comparison may be displayed only as an explicitly declared mixed-unit ledger, not as an ordinary physical sum.

Machine Guard [REVELATION ROSTER NON-COLLAPSE]:
Revelation 7’s tribal list and Revelation 14’s Lamb / Zion `144000` state are related but distinct textual states. Do not treat Revelation 14 as repeating the Revelation 7 roster.

Machine Guard [ZION TEXTUAL-TYPOLOGICAL DISTINCTION]:
Ezra 3 narrates the Second Temple foundation at Jerusalem; it does not itself use the phrase “Mount Zion” in that scene. Preserve Zion as the canonical-theological designation of the Jerusalem sanctuary field, not as a verbatim Ezra 3 quotation.

Machine Guard [CHRISTOLOGICAL CENSUS DATE-STATE]:
Luke 2 supplies the census, journey to Bethlehem, and city-of-David birth setting. `6 BC` is a repository Christological date-state controlled by `File_13`; do not claim that Luke textually supplies the year label.

Machine Guard [BETHLEHEM-ZION NON-COLLAPSE]:
Bethlehem is the city of David and the birthplace setting in Luke 2; it is not geographically identical with Mount Zion or the Jerusalem Temple Mount. Luke nevertheless carries the infant Jesus from Bethlehem to the Jerusalem Temple when the purification period is completed (Luke 2:22–38; Lev 12:2–4), supplying an explicit sanctuary / Zion sequence without geographical collapse.

Machine Guard [CHRISTOLOGICAL CAPSTONE NON-RAIL]:
The `6 BC` census-birth / Lamb state completes the Babel–Sinai–Zion theology but is not a fourth equal member of the historical `±910` rail unless a separate arithmetic derivation explicitly places it there.

Machine Guard [CROSS-SACRIFICIAL-HEIGHT RESTRAINT]:
The Gospels do not call Golgotha or the cross a mountain. “Ultimate sacrificial height” is a typological reading grounded in John’s “lifted up” language; preserve it as theological synthesis rather than textual geography.

Machine Guard [ASYMMETRICAL-1850 DECLARATION]:
`1850 = 1820 + 30` requires a one-sided `+30` anchor placement. Preserve `1790 = 1820 − 30` as the opposite cross-rail orientation; do not describe `1850` as the ordinary census difference.

Machine Guard [666000 BIDIRECTIONAL NON-MIRROR]:
The `666000` cross-axis projection is a count-to-chronology state. It is not automatically Protocol 1, Protocol 2, inverse-number architecture, or a formal Mirror unless a controlling dependency explicitly opens that protocol.

Machine Guard [KEY-OF-23 WHOLE-YEAR STATE]:
The `25/23` operation in §11 is an exact whole-year expansion. It is not the Residue Protocol and must not import residual-day logic.

Machine Guard [PROVIDENTIAL NON-AUTHORIAL]:
Exact remote closure may support providential synchronization or high-coherence structural inference. It does not by itself prove conscious authorial design across Numbers, Revelation, the SKL, and Berossus.

Machine Guard [NO PARTIAL-HIT PROOF]:
An individual tribe, running-total boundary, or remote target cannot carry proof weight unless the complete declared dataset, operator inventory, alternatives, and failures are reported.

Machine Guard [DEPENDENCY PROOF-BURDEN]:
`File_59` may cite SKL, Berossus, Rounded, Pillar, and restoration nodes by dependency but must not present their full derivation as local proof unless the controlling material is reproduced and verified.



Machine Guard [SOURCE-ORDER / MARCHING-ORDER HEAD FIREWALL]:
Reuben is first in the normalized Numbers 1 comparison order and heads the South camp; Judah heads the East camp and leads the march. Name the active order before assigning a quadrant-head function.

Machine Guard [REVELATION QUADRANT ALLOCATION]:
`36000 = 3 × 12000` is a schematic Revelation allocation for one three-tribe block. It does not replace any actual military census total or imply that Numbers itself assigns `12000` to each tribe.

Judah is Jacob’s fourth son overall. Issachar and Zebulun are Leah’s fifth and sixth sons, not Jacob’s fifth and sixth sons overall. Preserve the maternal and total birth-order distinctions.

`36000` is the complement of Judah’s `108000` remainder within `144000`. Do not call this relation an inverse; inverse-number architecture is a separate repository operator.

A local residual of `60` ordinary years and the macro-unit `21600 = 60 × 360` are related scale states, not the same unit. State the active scale before drawing a fractal comparison.

The exact Judah equation `453576 BC → AD 25 = 453600` lands at `AD 25`. `AD 30` remains an adjacent permitted Passion state under File_13 / File_16 controls but is not an alternate endpoint of the same equation.

### D.23.4 Claim-status and cross-file routing

- The textual Numbers 1 and Numbers 26 military totals and their exact arithmetic carry the primary burden. The `602640` mean, `1820` difference, and `±910` half-difference are derived arithmetic states, not additional textual censuses.
- The military-census formatting result `22/24 = 11/12` mod `100` is an arithmetic fact. It admits `22273 → 22300` as a nearest-hundred census display. The MT simultaneously preserves the stated Levitical total `22000` and the clan-row subtotal `22300 = 7500 + 8600 + 6200`. The rounded display and clan subtotal are independent provenances; neither replaces exact `22273` or official `22000`.
- The `270/273/300` kernel, `350/364/365` residuals, `1260/1274/1275` half-week family, `2520/2548/2550` full-week family, `22080 = 48 × 460`, and `579600 = 1260 × 460` are arithmetic facts once their state and inclusive operator are declared. Their coordination is structural inference.
- File_20 controls the derivative Jubilees 16 Isaac-gestation / `273`-animal comparison and Isaac age-`15` transfer; File_04 and File_16 control the `+215`, MT Minimum `−215`, and LXX `−33` chronology states; File_17 controls `1260/1274/1290`, `2520`, `2670/2700/2730`, and exact-ratio span grammar; File_55 controls the `1366 BC` Joshua terminal and related `460` context.
- The `−215`-comparison and LXX `−33` rails are reconciled without collapse: their distinct `1440` and `1290` translations converge at the same `606–536 BC` captivity-restoration field. The arithmetic establishes convergence; priestly redemption, differentiated unity, and unified divine authorship remain theological / providential interpretations.

### D.27.4 File_63-specific Machine Guards

The forty-six File_63-specific guards are reproduced below verbatim. The repository-standard Machine Guard `[FILE MAP RESTRAINT]` is already active elsewhere in this Register and is not duplicated here.


Machine Guard [FILE_63 FILE_62 FREEZE]:

`480` is the whole-year carrier, `483` the quarter-phase / sixty-nine-week carrier, and `490` the completed Prophetic / Jubilee carrier. A shared endpoint does not make them one span.

Machine Guard [FILE_63 TWELVE INTERVALS / THIRTEEN BOUNDARIES]:
`12×40=480` counts twelve completed intervals. A twelve-interval line has thirteen boundaries; the extra boundary is not automatically a thirteenth male.

Machine Guard [FILE_63 SCALE / CHRONOLOGY NON-COLLAPSE]:
Multiplication by `10` or division by `10` preserves scalar form. It does not identify historical dates, traditions, or node-classes.

Machine Guard [FILE_63 TRANSLATION NOTATION]:
`τ_s` is local shorthand for ordinary subtraction applied to a coordinate or complete envelope. It is not a repository-wide operator, Gear, clutch, Mirror, or Residue Protocol.

Machine Guard [FILE_63 PHASE JOINT RESTRAINT]:
A `t/n` or `n/t` state is a declared half-year calendar relation, not free chronological tolerance. Every phase equation must state whether it is same-phase or cross-phase.

Machine Guard [FILE_63 PRECISE / ROUNDED KINGS NON-COLLAPSE]:

Do not call the Rounded rail literal / actual, and do not move the precise rail by one year merely because the Rounded labels are cleaner. `966 BC` may simultaneously function as the cardinal historical Temple landing and as a mod-5-compatible Rounded coordinate; the shared label does not collapse the states.
