### D.27.5 Claim-status and cross-file routing

| Claim type | File_63 usage |
|---|---|
| Textual datum | Explicit biblical ages, reign lengths, months, counts, and narrative sequence. |
| Source-retained chronology datum | A coordinate or state imported from a controlling repository file. |
| Arithmetic fact | An exact calculation under a named operator. |
| Proposed phase state | A `t/n`, `n/t`, or transition coordinate not directly stated by the source. |
| Derived translation state | A coordinate generated by `τ_s` or another predeclared same-side arithmetic step. |
| Structural inference | Twelve-male ledger, Levitical thirteenth, priest–king midpoint, translation architecture. |
| Typological reading | Temple gestation; Jethro / Melchizedek comparison; royal-priestly action. |
| Plausible authorial-design hypothesis | Same-side BC integration that may reflect scribal construction. |
| Providential synchronization | BC/AD Mirror, AD rail landings, and precessional closure. |
| Dependency-controlled | Claim whose proof remains in a named source file. |
| Exploratory note | Secondary echo or generated member not needed for the proof spine. |
| Audit note | Item requiring later source, operator, or author-decision clarification. |

### D.28.4 File_64-specific Machine Guards

The forty-nine File_64-specific guards are reproduced below verbatim from the current pressure-tested Final source. The repository-standard Machine Guard `[FILE MAP RESTRAINT]` remains active elsewhere in this Register and is not duplicated here.

Machine Guard [FILE_64 HISTORICAL / COMPOSITIONAL NON-COLLAPSE]:
The complete Judges duration sum does not claim uninterrupted elapsed chronology. Historical overlap and compositional addition are simultaneous non-competing states.

Machine Guard [FILE_64 DURATION CORPUS BOUNDARY]:
The main duration corpus is Judges 3:8–16:31 and includes only expressly stated year durations assigned to subjugation, rest, rule, or judgeship. Do not import months, days, household counts, military counts, ages, or inferred reign lengths.

Machine Guard [FILE_64 JEPHTHAH 300 NON-ADDITION]:
Judges 11:26's `300` is the retrospective territorial key. It is not an additional row in the serial `410` duration ledger.

Machine Guard [FILE_64 SAMSON DUPLICATE-VERSE NON-DUPLICATION]:
Judges 15:20 and 16:31 restate the same twenty-year Samson judgeship. Count `20` once, not twice.

Machine Guard [FILE_64 SAMSON WITHIN PHILISTINE DAYS]:
The printed `40 + 20` display does not require sixty non-overlapping historical years. Samson's twenty years occur “in the days of the Philistines.”

Machine Guard [FILE_64 EAST / WEST JORDAN]:
`1407 BC` is the east-Jordan occupation head for Jephthah's territorial statement. `1406 BC` is the west-Jordan Conquest head. Do not collapse them.

Machine Guard [FILE_64 1107 / 1106 BOUNDARY]:
`1107 BC` is the local Jephthah speech/accession head used by the direct `91`-year route. `1106 BC` is the completed-boundary / cumulative harmonic companion preserved from File_06. Neither replaces the other.

Machine Guard [FILE_64 WHOLE-YEAR / PHASE NON-COLLAPSE]:
Unsuffixed whole-year equations do not automatically establish exact `t/n` phase coordinates. Appendix B controls the original Conquest projections; Appendix D controls the Northern comparison routes `963n → 722t = 241.5` and `963t → 721n = 241.5`. The whole-year label `963 BC` remains a comparison shorthand.

Machine Guard [FILE_64 EXODUS / GREAT-NEXUS NON-COLLAPSE]:
`1446 BC` is the repository's primary textually derived Exodus anchor. `1876 BC` is the great Genesis Nexus / Entry or variant-convergence label. Do not call `1876 BC` the Exodus.

Machine Guard [FILE_64 ANCHORED SCHEMATIC / EXTERNAL-HISTORICITY]:
A schematic ledger must be tied to a named textual or historical head / terminus. That internal anchoring does not by itself prove the modern external historicity of every repository anchor, including `1446 BC`.

In particular, `1396 BC` is a derived matrix coordinate, `1016 BC` is schematic, and `966 BC` is historical. Do not promote the whole lattice to one historical chronology.

Machine Guard [FILE_64 JACOB LATTICE NON-DRIVER]:

### D.28.5 Claim-status and proof-burden control

| Claim-status label | Function in `File_64` |
|---|---|
| Textual datum | A duration, sequence, place, or narrative statement expressly supplied by the biblical text. |
| Source-retained chronology datum | A date or phase state imported from a controlling repository file. |
| Arithmetic fact | An exact sum, difference, product, ratio, or factorization under a named operator. |
| Structural inference | A pattern inferred from repeated state-controlled arithmetic. |
| Plausible scribal-design hypothesis | A bounded same-book or same-corpus arrangement that may reflect deliberate composition. |
| Typological reading | A correspondence grounded in the canonical narrative, such as Joshua–David giant conflict. |
| Literary-theological synthesis | The interpretation of Judges disorder through later Jerusalem, kingship, ark, and Temple resolution. |
| Providential synchronization | A coherent alignment extending beyond demonstrable human authorial access. |
| Dependency-controlled | A claim whose full proof remains in a named repository file. |
| Appendix-only | A valid but non-load-bearing calculation excluded from the primary proof. |
| Exploratory note | A secondary observation retained for testing. |
| Audit note | A source, arithmetic, operator, or state issue requiring explicit handling. |

Primary-burden rule:
The main proof rests on Judges 11:26, the complete bounded Judges duration corpus, source-order arithmetic, category totals, and the independently controlled Exodus / Conquest anchors. The Jacob final-`70` lattice, Jacob–Daniel closure, Cainan–Terah / Judges rails, royal excess ledgers, paired `930` construction rails, Bethel–Adam typology, double-`400` companions, triple-`460`, census palindromes, and Flood corroboration are downstream explanations of the already derived `1016 BC` Nexus. None may be used to generate the Judges coordinate. The later AD 135 envelope, appendix category projections, and additional Key-of-23 scans do not carry the base proof.

### D.29.2 File_65-specific Machine Guards

Machine Guard [FILE_65 LOCAL CONTROL / PARENT CONTROL]:

`File_65` controls only its local evidence inventory, derived comparison states, and claim-status assignments. Parent files control inherited anchors, source rows, operators, and chronology states. A local synthesis may compare those states but must not redefine them.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 A7 PRIMARY / A6 AUDIT]:

Athaliah's seventh-year boundary is the primary verbatim / schematic Judah-ledger state and produces the leading `50`-year surplus. The six-completed-year state remains a subordinate textual-duration audit producing `49`. A6 must not be allowed to displace A7 merely because both participate in the `299/300` seam, and A7 must not be restated as seven completed reign years.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 AHAZIAH–ATHALIAH DYNASTIC SEAM]:

Ahaziah's one-year reign and Athaliah's six-completed / seventh-year-boundary states remain distinct textual reign states. They may form one schematic Omride-intrusion boundary because the narrative presents Ahaziah as governed by the house of Ahab and Athaliah as continuing that regime after his death. This does not make Athaliah a legitimate extension of Ahaziah's Davidic reign and does not alter either ruler's source duration.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 TERMINAL QUARTER-REIGN PHASE]:

Jehoahaz's three months and Jehoiachin's three months are distributed across `(11 + 0.25) + (11 + 0.25) = 22.5`. Their combined half-year carries the Kings ledger from Tishri to Nisan. It remains outside the whole-year `299 × 300/299` operation. Chronicles' additional ten days remain a separate textual payload.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 ATHALIAH–AHAZIAH `299/300` BOUNDARY]:

Preserve the post-Athaliah subtotal `292`, Athaliah's six-completed-year state, her seventh-year boundary state, and Ahaziah's one-year reign as distinct inputs. The simultaneous decompositions are `292 + 7 = 299` and `292 + 6 + 1 = 299`. The exact completion is `299 × 300/299 = 300`. Do not convert the completed value into an ordinary seven-year reign for Athaliah or a two-year reign for Ahaziah.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 `886n BC` SCHEMATIC NODE]:

`886n BC` is the completed Nisan-state head of the reverse Judah ledger. The terminal quarter-reign payload first resolves the local terminal from `586t` to the ledger's `586n` state; the whole-year operation then executes `885n − 586n = 299` and `886n − 586n = 300`. The half-year remains outside `299 × 300/299`. The coordinate is schematic and must not be represented as the historical accession date of Ahaziah or Athaliah.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 NET SURPLUS / CALENDAR PAYLOAD]:

The primary A7 phase-resolved Judah surplus is `50` years, with Chronicles adding `10` days. The subordinate A6 surplus is `49` years, with the same ten-day increment. The `180/190`-day part-reign totals are source-retained calendar payloads. They may map to Tishri 1 / Tishri 10 under the declared `360`-day schematic calendar, but they must not be reported as uncancelled portions of the phase-resolved net surplus.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 REGULAR-MT `1290` / LOCALIZED-LXX RAIL]:

`1876 BC → 586 BC = 1290` belongs to the regular-MT Jacob Entry / Triple-`430` core. The localized LXX `−33` rail supplies the flanking `1896 → 606` and `1826 → 536` columns. Do not say that regular MT lacks a `1290` event merely because the untranslated standard final-life rail does not generate the two flanking columns.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 TRIPLE-`430` LITERAL / SCHEMATIC]:

The first `430`, `1876 → 1446 BC`, is the literal MT Sojourn. The second and third `430`s use the schematic `1016 BC` pivot. Equality of measure does not make all three segments the same node-class or proof type.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 CROSS-TRADITION `1876 BC` CONTINUITY]:

At `1876 BC`, regular MT may mark Jacob's Entry, localized LXX may mark Jacob's return, and SP may mark Abraham's call. These are continuity witnesses on a shared year-label, not one collapsed historical event. The regular-MT Entry remains the central `1290` source state.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 `7/13` PIVOT NON-OVERCOUNT]:

The relations `1406 → 886n = 40 × 13`, `886n → 606 = 40 × 7`, `886n → 536 = 5 × 70`, and `1016 → 886n → 586 = 130 + 300` are consequences of one independently generated `886n BC` node. They form one integrated Reconciliation-Fractal structure and must not be counted as separate proofs of the node.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 THIRDS CORROBORATION / SCRIBAL RESTRAINT]:

The exact `1446 → 886n → 606 = 560 + 280 = 840` partition places `886n BC` at the remaining-two-thirds / removed-one-third joint only after the Judah ledger independently generates that node. File_55 controls the thirds operator. File_65 classifies the resulting alignment as cross-file structural corroboration or providential synchronization, not as evidence that the Judah scribe consciously executed the complete File_55 system.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 JEHOIACHIN `561/562` STATE]:

`561 BC` is the preferred local historical comparison for Jehoiachin's elevation. `562/561 BC` remains the accession adjacency field and `562 BC` remains an audit alternative. The exact `20 + 25 + 25 + 20` symmetry may discriminate internally between states but must not replace the historical source argument.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 EZEKIEL SIEGE PHASE PAIR]:

`589t/588n BC` is one January-centered phase pair for the tenth-month siege opening. It is not a range, average, or pair of competing dates. File_17's unsuffixed `588 BC` remains the Nisan/default shorthand.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 EZEKIEL 33:21 APPARATUS]:

BHS prints the MT twelfth-year reading. Its apparatus records an eleventh-year alternative in a few Hebrew manuscripts, the Lucianic Greek tradition, and Syriac. The apparatus does not “recommend” the twelfth year. File_65 may retain the MT reading as the active state and may argue that it fits the chronology, but it must preserve the competing textual tradition and classify harder-reading language as text-critical inference.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 LUKE JUBILEE LEXICAL / LITERARY]:

LXX Leviticus 25 uses `ἄφεσις` for Jubilee release and `λύτρωσις` / cognates for redemption. Luke 2:38 uses `λύτρωσις`; Luke 4:18 uses `ἄφεσις` and proclaims the acceptable year. The lexical correspondences are textual data. Reading the two Lucan scenes as coordinated halves of one Jubilee mechanism is literary inference, and broader Luke–Jubilee interpretation remains debated.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 NISAN DEFAULT / PHASE ECONOMY]:

Use Nisan by default. Open a Tishri/Nisan pair only when the half-year or seasonal position carries proof burden. A valid phase pair must not be expanded into a general uncertainty range or imported into unrelated coordinates.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 `72`-YEAR APPENDIX ONLY]:

The `72`-year generation remains recorded-not-adopted. Its quotient identity, precessional comparisons, `AD 31` half-generation endpoint, `725 BC` landing, and author-directed `AD 34n/34t` shadow do not support the main Luke `70`-year rail and must remain in Appendix C.

Machine Guard [FILE_65 PASS-2 ARITHMETIC STATUS]:

The Final arithmetic manifest and the independent Pass 2 re-execution each evaluated `257` rows and returned `257/257 PASS`. This total replaces source-v7's superseded `146/146` claim. Pass 2 is a Verification Pass, not a full post-final pressure test; no pressure-tested status may be inferred.

### D.29.4 Claim-status and dependency boundary

| Claim type | File_65 usage |
|---|---|
| Textual datum | Reign notices, date strings, lexical forms, and source durations. |
| Source-retained chronology datum | Parent-controlled Jacob, Exodus, Kings, Nexus, exile, Luke, and Ezekiel states. |
| Arithmetic fact | Exact results under declared same-side, cross-axis, phase, ledger, and rational operators. |
| Conditional arithmetic fact | Exact result after a preferred or explicitly selected historical / phase state is admitted. |
| Strong structural inference | Reverse Judah pincer; coordinated `40 × 13 → 40 × 7` pivot; constrained cross-file integration. |
| Plausible scribal-design hypothesis | Same-book / same-ledger architecture whose conscious design is possible but not demonstrated. |
| Typological / literary-theological reading | Canonical meaning that does not carry the arithmetic proof burden. |
| Providential synchronization | Exact cross-file coherence, especially the File_55 thirds landing, beyond demonstrated scribal intent. |
| Appendix-only / exploratory | `72`-year shadow, `AD 34n/34t`, and other retained non-main-proof comparisons. |
| Dependency-controlled | Claim whose proof remains in a named parent file. |

- A7 is primary for the schematic Judah ledger; A6 remains the completed-duration audit.
- The terminal half-year is resolved separately from the whole-year `299/300` operation.
- `886n BC` is a File_65-local schematic head, not an accession date or repository Primary anchor.
- `1876 → 586 = 1290` is the regular-MT central Triple-`430`; localized LXX supplies flanking columns without creating the center.
- The `40 × 13 → 40 × 7` pivot is a strong structural inference; conscious scribal design remains plausible rather than proved.
- The File_55 thirds alignment remains providential / corroborative, not a new File_55 proof.
- No parent-file source duration, historical anchor, operator, Bullseye category, or proof spine is altered.

### D.30.1A Additional File_66 terminology controls — August 5–7 amendments

#### 0.7.17 MT Adjusted / SP-215 terminology

The File_66 comparison row `1951/1876/1851/1776 BC` belongs to the inherited whole-biography `−215` translation state. It is not the Samaritan Pentateuch’s only possible chronology and does not replace the regular Abraham/Isaac anchors. `1851 BC` is a File_66-derived Isaac-birth member, calculated from `2066 − 215`; it is not a printed biblical date.

#### 0.7.18 Jeremiah pre-Fall ministry terminology

Use `626 BC` as File_66’s preferred whole-year head for Jeremiah’s call in Josiah’s thirteenth year, with `627/626 BC` retained as the external chronology tolerance. The local `626 → 586 = 40` describes the pre-Fall warning field. Jeremiah 1:3 and the subsequent narrative show that Jeremiah’s prophetic activity continued through the Fall and afterward; do not call the `40` his total lifetime ministry.

#### 0.7.19 Ramesside-origin hypothesis terminology

Use **Ramesside-origin hypothesis** for the Appendix F proposal that `1231 BC` may preserve the latent historical Exodus horizon while `1446 BC` remains the primary canonical chronographic horizon. The phrase does not reverse the repository hierarchy outside Appendix F.

#### 0.7.20 Canonical / historical priority terminology

**Canonical priority** names the repository's operative `1446 BC` anchor. **Hypothesized historical priority** names the Appendix F proposal concerning `1231 BC`. The two priority claims are distinct and may coexist.

#### 0.7.21 Retrojection terminology

**Retrojection** names the arithmetic/chronographic movement `1231 + 215 = 1446` in BC labels. It does not by itself assert physical retrocausation, a rewritten past, or an independently demonstrated scribal mechanism.

#### 0.7.22 Three-precessional-day terminology

`215 = 3 × 71⅔` is the alternate exact date-fitting state; `216 = 3 × 72` is the standard companion. Their doubles are `430/432`. The text joins the third day and the fifteen years in one oracle but does not equate their units.

#### 0.7.23 Translation / pincer terminology

A **uniform translation** advances every corresponding coordinate by the same `215`. A **fixed-center pincer dilation** moves the two endpoints outward in opposite directions around `1016 BC`, doubling the radius from `215` to `430`. Do not use the terms interchangeably.

#### 0.7.24 Mirror-closure terminology

**Mirror closure** means the bounded test of whether an independently established same-side structure preserves its complete interval anatomy across the opposite BC/AD polarity. In File_66 it is required for whole-system closure but remains local Mirror corroboration rather than a new proof premise.

#### 0.7.25 Paired-arm terminology

`1290 + 1290 = 2580` and `1296 + 1296 = 2592` are paired Mirror-arm counts. They retain two coordinated cross-axis arms and must not be narrated as one uninterrupted chronological span.

#### 0.7.26 Deportation-envelope terminology

The `606/605–581/580 BC` field is an adjacent-state envelope. Its two diagonal widths are `25`; its inner and outer widths are `24/26`. The arithmetic fit does not determine the external historical status of every member.

#### 0.7.27 `427`-corridor terminology

`427` is the derived left boundary of the palindromic multiplier sequence `427/429/430/432 = 2|1|2`. The cumulative offsets `2/3/5` are consequences of that symmetry. Neither `427` nor `183610` becomes a Primary chronology anchor or an independently sacred number.

#### 0.7.28 Latent-relay terminology

A **latent relay** is a mathematically required division point without an assigned historical event. `186/156 BC` are the lower rail's reciprocal `1260/1290` stations and remain unused as history.

#### 0.7.29 Contracted / uncontracted overlap terminology

The **contracted elapsed union** counts the common `215` once and yields `6.5 × 430 = 13 × 215`. The **uncontracted two-arm structure** retains the common `215` in each `3.5 × 430` arm and yields `7 × 430 = 14 × 215`. Only the first is the ordinary outer elapsed span.

#### 0.7.30 Precessional-month / intercalation terminology

`2520/2550/2580` represent a seven-year prophetic body with zero, one, or two added `30`-day months. At the alternate/standard precessional scales, each added month becomes `2150/2160`. This is a scale-state relation and does not prescribe one historical calendar.

#### 0.7.31 Center, midpoint coordinate, pair-sum, half-span, pivot, hub, and overlap terminology

File_66 uses these terms in distinct mathematical senses:

- A **fixed same-side center** is a date-coordinate equidistant from two endpoints on one BC or AD rail. `1016 BC`, `976 BC`, and `711 BC` are examples where equal arms are displayed.
- A **civil-linear midpoint coordinate** is obtained only after the endpoints have been mapped to a continuous no-year-zero coordinate line. For civil `B BC`, use `L = 1 − B`; for `AD D`, use `L = D`. `693.5 BC` is the File_66 example.
- A **complementary pair-sum** identifies equal endpoint-label sums. A same-side label average may be mathematically valid, but it is not automatically a Mirror node or a load-bearing chronology coordinate.
- A **civil span** is an elapsed BC/AD duration computed by `BC + AD − 1`; its **half-span** is a duration, not a year label.
- A **pivot** is a structurally important coordinate that need not be equidistant from its endpoints. A6 `1015 BC` is an audit pivot, not a center.
- A **hub** is a value through which several arithmetic relations pass. It need not be a chronological midpoint.
- A **central segment** or **shared overlap interval** is an interval, not a point-center.

Ordinary statistical means and ledger averages retain their normal mathematical meaning and must state their units.

The controlling examples are:

```text
fixed same-side center:        1016 BC in 1446→1016→586 = 430+430
schematic companion center:    976 BC in 1366→976→586 = 390+390
civil-linear midpoint:         693.5 BC after no-year-zero linearization
A6 audit pivot:                1015 BC in 431|429
arithmetic hub:                185000
shared overlap interval:       801 BC→586 BC = 215
civil half-spans:              1296 = 648+648.
```

#### 0.7.32 Providential narrative-occupancy terminology

A **providentially occupied latent node** is an independently generated coordinate whose thematic position is filled by an undated canonical event of the corresponding narrative class. Occupancy may support strong structural inference and providential synchronization; it does not retroactively date the event, create an exact duration, or prove conscious scribal encoding.

### D.30.2 File_66-specific Machine Guards

Machine Guard [FILE_66 LOCAL / PARENT CONTROL]:
`File_66` controls only its local synthesis, state register, comparison coordinates, statistical execution, and claim-status assignments. Parent files retain control of inherited anchors, operators, and proof spines.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 ACTUAL / VERBATIM NON-COLLAPSE]:
Actual/Historical and Verbatim/Schematic dates may share labels or overlap, but they are not one chronology unless state, node-class, and operator agree.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 BIOGRAPHICAL NODE-CLASS]:
Hezekiah birth, accession, fourteenth-year crisis, and death are distinct nodes. Do not transfer an operator from one node to another without stating the active rail.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 BIRTH-LABEL RESTRAINT]:
`750 BC` and `740 BC` are derived whole-year birth labels generated from accession labels plus the textual age `25`. They are not exact natal dates.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 TWO CO-REGENCIES]:
The Hezekiah–Ahaz and Hezekiah–Manasseh co-regencies are distinct reconstructions with different directions and functions. Do not combine them into one generic overlap.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 AGE-25 / AGE-11]:
The textual age `25` governs Hezekiah’s formal sole accession. The age-approximately-`11` junior co-regency is a historical reconstruction and does not rewrite 2 Chronicles 29:1.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 10 / 11 CO-REGENCY]:
A `10`-year elapsed overlap and an `11`-regnal-year inclusive overlap may describe the same preferred `696 → 686 BC` field. Do not treat them as competing arithmetic without naming the counting convention.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 697 / 696 MANASSEH]:
`697 BC` remains the secondary McFall-refined Thiele-associated Manasseh co-regency label. `696 BC` is the author-preferred File_66 refined comparison state. Neither is promoted beyond its declared reconstruction status before audit.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 696 CROSS-LEDGER SEAM]:
The overlap of Actual Manasseh onset and Verbatim Hezekiah death at `696 BC` is a symbolic cross-ledger seam. It does not make the two reigns, events, or persons identical.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 MANASSEH 641 / 642]:
`641 BC` is the Verbatim/Schematic next boundary produced by adding Manasseh’s `55` whole ledger years to the schematic `696 BC` head. `642 BC` is the preferred Actual/Historical death boundary under the File_66 `54` elapsed / `55` inclusive-regnal-year comparison. Do not collapse these adjacent boundaries or use either in the other ledger without declaring the state.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 MIXED-EPOCH SYNCHRONISM]:
The historically harmonized reading of 2 Kings 18 counts the Samaria notices from the Hezekiah–Ahaz co-regency and the Judah notice from Hezekiah’s sole reign. Do not force all notices onto one epoch as ordinary history.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 715 / 701 JERUSALEM]:
`715 BC` is a latent Year-14 coordinate generated from the co-regency epoch. `701 BC` remains the historical Sennacherib–Judah crisis and the controlling illness/deliverance hinge.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 711 / SAMARIA]:
`711 BC` is a latent Year-4 coordinate generated from the sole-reign epoch and the Verbatim Jerusalem crisis. It is not the historical beginning of Samaria’s siege.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 10 / 14 NON-COLLAPSE]:
The `10`-year horizontal interval between Years 4 and 14 and the `14`-year vertical shift between co-regency and sole-reign epochs are distinct operators.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 A7 / A6]:
A7 is the primary Verbatim/Schematic Judah state and yields the `50`-year package. A6 is a completed-duration sensitivity state and must not replace A7.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 TEMPLE STATES]:
Historical `967/966 BC` and schematic `1016 BC` are distinct Temple states. The A6 `1015 BC` coordinate is audit-only.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 115 / 125]:
`115` is the historical `701 → 586 BC` city delay. `125` is the Priestly-Key completion generated by `25/23`. Do not collapse raw and completed arms.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 165 / 175]:
`165 = 50 + 115` and `175 = 50 + 125` are composite backward-plus-forward fields. They are not ordinary uninterrupted date spans and are not competing corrections.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 2081 STATUS]:
`2081 BC` is an author-designated Covenant-of-Pieces comparison coordinate controlled by File_63. It is not an explicit date supplied by Genesis.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 CHILD NON-COLLAPSE]:
Isaiah 7’s Immanuel child and Isaiah 8’s Maher-shalal-hash-baz are distinct surface-text identities. File_66 may analyze them as a coordinated child-sign complex but must not silently merge them into one biographical person.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 735 / 734 STATE]:
`735 BC` is the controlled prophecy/conception or sign-head state; `734 BC` is the birth display. Do not collapse or average the pair.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 CHILD AGE / SIGN SPAN]:
`735 → 723/722 BC = 12/13` is a sign-head span, not automatically the child’s exact biological age.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 BAR-MITZVAH RESTRAINT]:
Age `13` may be compared with later Jewish legal maturity only as reception-history corroboration. It is not proof of Isaiah’s intended age threshold.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 EARLY / FINAL SAMARIA]:
The early Assyrian spoil/deportation stage and the final Samaria fall field are distinct historical stages. Do not collapse them into one event.

Machine Guard [FILE_66 SISTER-CITY OVERLAY]:
The Samaria–Jerusalem coordinate overlap does not make the two sieges one historical event. It is a crossed literary-theological comparison between sister cities under Assyrian judgment.

### D.30.4 Claim-status and dependency boundary

| Claim-status | Function in `File_66` |
|---|---|
| Textual datum | Biblical age, reign notice, month notice, sign statement, threat, prayer, deliverance promise, or named city/king relation. |
| Source-retained chronology datum | Date or reconstruction inherited from a parent repository file. |
| Reconstructed chronology state | A co-regency, accession, or synchronism proposed to harmonize regnal notices and external chronology. |
| Author-proposed historical-chronographic hypothesis | Appendix-only inversion that distinguishes a candidate historical event horizon from the repository's canonical chronographic anchor. |
| Arithmetic fact | Exact calculation under declared coordinates and operators. |
| Conditional arithmetic fact | Exact once a proposed reconstruction or phase state is admitted. |
| Statistical result | Defined output of the whole-year contiguous-span scan or declared robustness null. |
| Strong structural inference | Multiple constrained textual and arithmetic features interlock under a declared state. |
| Plausible scribal-design hypothesis | The received ambiguity or ordering appears purposeful, but conscious design is not demonstrable. |
| Typological reading | David/Hezekiah, king/city, sister-city, Abrahamic, or Joshua reversal interpretation. |
| Literary-theological synthesis | Canonical meaning joining related narratives without making theology carry the arithmetic proof. |
| Providential synchronization | Exact cross-file coherence beyond demonstrable human intention. |
| Mirror coordinate-completion | The opposite-polarity execution is required to close the whole-system coordinate field without carrying the local proof. |
| Mirror corroboration | Appendix G stress-tests and confirms a structure already established from textual, chronological, and same-side evidence. |
| Dependency-controlled | Parent file or external chronology source retains proof burden. |
| Appendix-only | Useful comparison that does not carry the main proof. |
| Exploratory note | Insufficiently constrained for the primary argument. |
| Audit note | Source, attribution, or statistical detail reserved for post-Pass-1 audit. |

- Files 01, 05, 08, 16, 19, 26, and 65 retain their parent proof burdens under the reciprocal guards above; File_06 required no source change.

### D.32.2 File_68 operator and state grammar

- Textual weeks, ordinary `70/72` carriers, seven-year envelopes, and generation-resolved `180/182` cells remain distinct.
- `τ_110` is the exact File_68-position → translated-rail operator; Cainan expunction changes row attribution only.
- The three accordion resolutions use `1`, `7`, and `49` copies of `+110`; they are measurement resolutions, not competing chronologies.
- Generated full-resolution heads `8756/8854/8936/9036 BC` and fully stretched Week-2 boundaries `6236/6306 BC` are local generated coordinates, never repository Primary anchors.
- `AD 65` remains the sole translated carrier terminus; `AD 71/72` remain terminal-envelope members and `AD 67` remains retired.
- The finite Sanctuary matrix preserves the interval vector `20|50` through the declared `1290→1260→1150` contractions; its central triples are mixed adjacent-row fields.
- The `720/728/730` triptych preserves Prophetic `360`, Enochian `364`, and rounded `365` without collapsing rounded solar with true-solar `365.2422`.
- The MT-contoured corridor, Cainan-retained/expunged overlays, Joshua I/Joshua II, and boundary-inheritance grammar remain File_68-specific applied states.
- Mirror, SKL/Berossus, cumulative, Decimal-Invariance, and Near-Solar material remain dependency-controlled and subordinate to the local proof hierarchy.

Machine Guard [FILE_68 TEXTUAL WEEK / DUAL YEAR CARRIERS]:

Both translated carrier tables terminate at `AD 65`. The final `70`-year generation is `6 BC → AD 65`; the final `72`-year generation is `8 BC → AD 65`. No `AD 67` terminal state is active. The phase-resolved `AD 64t/65n–AD 71t/72n` envelope remains distinct.

### D.32.5 Claim-status and dependency boundary

### D.33.2 File_69 operator and state grammar

The fixed terminal and count grammar are:

```markdown
File_68 Lukan corresponding-boundary state = 76 carrier cells
File_69 Lukan full-outer state              = 77 carrier cells
Matthew composite                          = 21 + 42 = 63 carrier cells
Enochic full-outer state                    = 1 + 49 = 50 carrier cells
common ordinary terminal                   = AD 65
second-generation closes                   = AD 135 / AD 137
```

For ordinary carrier `g∈{70,72}` and exact rail `r=110`, the invariant local cell is:

```markdown
same-rail generation       = g
rail separation            = r
internal overlap           = r − g
outer cell                 = g + r = 180 / 182
ordinary week              = 7g = 490 / 504
week-resolved diagonal     = 7g + r = 600 / 614
fully stretched week       = 7(g + r) = 1260 / 1274
```

The count fingerprint and Mosaic branch are:

```markdown
external Luke → Matthew → Enochic = 14 + 13 = 27
internal Adam → second Cainan → Moses = 13 + 14 = 27
neighboring attribution states = 26 / 27 / 28
primary ladder   = 3366 → 2876 → 2386 → 1896 → 1406 BC
companion ladder = 3464 → 2960 → 2456 → 1952 → 1448 BC
```

Each adjacent ladder step is one carrier week; each two-step window is fourteen generations. Only the primary ladder closes on the historical `1406 BC` Moses-death / Conquest anchor. File_69's mechanistic claim is that coordinates are generated from declared counts, carrier, terminal, rail, and resolution before thematic comparison. “Fractal-like” means discrete recurrence under those operators, not unrestricted scaling or continuous geometric fractal dimension.

### D.33.3 File_69 Machine Guards

Machine Guard [FILE_69 FILE_68 NON-REPLACEMENT]:
`File_69` extends Final File_68 into Luke and Matthew. It does not alter File_68's seven-week proof, `AD 65` terminal, `70/72` carriers, `110` rail, Cainan attribution, or Joshua corridor.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 LUKE `76/77` NON-COLLAPSE]:
File_68's corresponding-boundary Lukan state is `76×70`: God head to `6 BC`, Adam head to `AD 65`, and the next boundary to `AD 135`. File_69's full-outer state is `77×70`: God head to `AD 65`. Do not use one count without its endpoint pair.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 MATTHEW `41/42` NON-COLLAPSE]:
Matthew contains `41` distinct names under the active count and `42` occupied slots only under the Jeconiah exile-overlap model. Do not state that the text contains forty-two distinct names.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 GOD BOOKEND]:
“God” is a figurative genealogical bookend from Luke 3:38. No date in this file is a date for divine origin, birth, or age.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 COMMON TERMINAL]:
`AD 65` is the common ordinary terminal. `AD 135` and `AD 137` are second-generation closes. They must not replace the terminal or be used to duplicate Matthew's exile slot.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 CIVIL CROSS-AXIS]:
All BC-to-AD civil spans use `BC + AD − 1`. Same-side BC spans use ordinary subtraction.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 RAIL DIRECTION]:
A forward-time movement in BC lowers the numeric year label. Displays such as `+110` identify the earlier BC-coordinate rail; do not confuse directional description with ordinary signed subtraction.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 RESOLUTION NON-COLLAPSE]:
`70/72`, `180/182`, and `600/614` are distinct carrier or resolution states. A generated full-resolution coordinate must not replace an ordinary genealogical coordinate.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 LOCAL GENERATION]:
Within a primary four-point cell the same-rail Enoch birth-to-ascension span remains `70`; within a strict companion cell it remains `72`. The outer cell is `180/182` only because it crosses the exact `110` rail.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 STRICT / TRANSPORTED COMPANION]:
A `72`-system accumulated displacement applied to a retained local `70`-year Enoch cell is a transported-primary-cell overlay. It is not a strict local `72`-year generation.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 CAINAN DUALITY]:
Regular restored Cainan `+130` and cumulative restored Cainan `+460` are distinct operators. Retained and expunged attribution states do not alter the fixed date grid.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 BJ NODE-CLASS]:
`3856 BC` is BJ Creation in Files 20/53 and Matthew's no-rail Enoch head in File_69. The shared label is a cross-tradition overlap, not one collapsed event.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 SUPER-CREATION NODE-CLASS]:

Machine Guard [FILE_69 CALENDAR NON-COLLAPSE]:
Prophetic `360`, Enochian `364`, and Priestly `336` are distinct registers. Equal endpoints or factorizations do not make them one calendar.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 APPARENT-AGE LOCALITY]:

Machine Guard [FILE_69 EXODUS HIERARCHY]:

Machine Guard [FILE_09 5520 PHASE-RESOLVED MIRROR]:

The source line `4114 BC + AD 1406 = 5520 years` is a compressed source display. The inherited active form is the phase-resolved Mirror coordinate-completion:

```markdown
4115t/4114n BC → AD 1406t/1407n = 5520 years
4115 + 1406 − 1 = 5520
4114 + 1407 − 1 = 5520
```

Do not return the file to the unsuffixed coordinate-sum explanation unless the author explicitly reverses this decision.

Machine Guard [FILE_09 AD 1406 PRECISE MIRROR NODE]:

`AD 1406` is the precise numeric-year Mirror node of the unsuffixed `1406 BC` Conquest:

```markdown
1406 + 1406 − 1 = 2811
```

It is not the Rounded / Protocol 1 target `AD 1405`, where:

```markdown
1406 + 1405 − 1 = 2810
```

Machine Guard [FILE_09 CONQUEST PHASE PAIR]:

`1407t/1406n BC` is the Conquest-side phase pair. `1407t BC` marks the East side of Jordan Conquest start and occupation by the 2.5 tribes. `1406n BC` marks the later West side crossing and occupation by the other 10 tribes. Aaron died shortly before `1407t BC`; Moses died shortly before `1406n BC`; each was mourned 30 days. Preserve the approximately six-month gap.

Machine Guard [FILE_09 MIRROR SUFFIX INVERSION]:

Phase-resolved `.5` Tishri/Nisan dual dates invert their suffix display across the Mirror in order to preserve the half-year phase distance.

Generic form:

```markdown
Yt/(Y−1)n BC → AD (Y−1)t/Y n
```

Therefore:

```markdown
1407t/1406n BC → AD 1406t/1407n
1447t/1446n BC → AD 1446t/1447n
```

Do not use the same-order AD display `AD (Y−1)n/Yt` for a phase-resolved Mirror pair unless a local section explicitly opens a different phase-state. The same-order AD display expands the AD-side internal phase distance to about `1.5` years rather than preserving the source pair's `.5` relation.

Proof-only phase notation:

```markdown
n = 0
t = 0.5
```

The whole-year civil skeleton remains:

```markdown
BC + AD − 1
```

When an exact phase-state is opened, compare source phase `p` with target phase `q`. Valid paired suffix inversion gives `q − p = 0` on both components. Same-order AD display gives one component with `q − p = −0.5` and the other with `q − p = +0.5`. That is why exact day/month calculations fail under the same-order display even if the integer labels look superficially balanced.

Example rejected form:

```markdown
1447t/1446n BC → AD 1446n/1447t
```

The AD-side pair `AD 1446n/1447t` is about `1.5` years apart. Therefore it is not the Mirror of the `.5` phase-pair `1447t/1446n BC`.

`AD 1406t/1407n` reflects the Conquest-side pair `1407t/1406n BC` by phase-resolved Mirror coordinate-completion. The AD-side display is intentionally ordered by Mirror relation and suffix inversion. Do not collapse it into unsuffixed `AD 1406` or treat it as a Rounded / Protocol 1 mod-5 target.

Machine Guard [FILE_09 JULIAN JAN-1 PHASE MODEL]:

For the simplified Julian phase-coordinate proof, use:

```markdown
Oct 1 = Tishri 1 = `t`
Apr 1 = Nisan 1 = `n`
Jan 1 = Mirror-axis / center point
```

The repository's phase-resolved paired Mirror state is January-centered:

```markdown
Yt/(Y−1)n BC → AD (Y−1)t/Y n
```

The same-year display `Yn/Yt` is July-centered and is not the active Creation / Conquest paired Mirror state. The July-centered `n/t` form must not replace the Jan-1-centered `t/n` form in exact month/day work.

The simplified Julian phase-coordinate model is a proof model for repository phase logic. It does not open astronomical month-length corrections unless a local day-count section explicitly opens that state.

Machine Guard [FILE_09 PAIRED / SINGLE MIRROR DATES]:

Single exact Mirror dates and paired phase-resolved Mirror dates are distinct states.

Single-date checks:

```markdown
1407t BC → AD 1407t
1407 + 1407 − 1 = 2813

1406n BC → AD 1406n
1406 + 1406 − 1 = 2811
```

Paired phase-resolved Mirror check:

```markdown
1407t/1406n BC → AD 1406t/1407n
1407 + 1406 − 1 = 2812
1406 + 1407 − 1 = 2812
```

The paired value `2812` is the average of the two non-paired single-date Mirror spans:

```markdown
2813 + 2811 = 5624
5624 ÷ 2 = 2812
```

This average explains why the paired state sits between the two single-date exact Mirror spans. It must not be turned into a general slash-pair averaging operator. The active paired calculation remains component-wise civil cross-axis arithmetic after suffix inversion.

Machine Guard [FILE_09 STANDALONE POLARITY FLIP]:

Standalone exact Mirror dates and paired phase-resolved Mirror dates must remain separate.

A standalone Tishri date mirrored to its own AD numeric-year counterpart yields an odd span:

```markdown
Yt BC → AD Yt
Y + Y − 1 = 2Y − 1
```

A standalone Nisan date mirrored to its own AD numeric-year counterpart also yields an odd span:

```markdown
(Y−1)n BC → AD (Y−1)n
(Y−1) + (Y−1) − 1 = 2Y − 3
```

Because these spans are odd, each standalone single-date midpoint is a half-span. In exact phase work, that half-span flips phase polarity at the midpoint: a Tishri-to-Tishri single-date Mirror has a Nisan-side midpoint, and a Nisan-to-Nisan single-date Mirror has a Tishri-side midpoint.

This is not an inconsistency. It is a different modal state from the paired `t/n` Mirror. In `File_09`, the Jan-1 Mirror-axis proof applies to the paired `t/n` state as an average / center-state. Full treatment of standalone Nisan or Tishri polarity inversion should be controlled by a future Mirror-mechanics file, likely `File_51b` or a repository glossary entry.

Formal necessity proof for the Conquest pair:

```markdown
Valid paired display:
1407t BC → AD 1406t = 2812
1406n BC → AD 1407n = 2812

Invalid same-order AD display:
1407t BC → AD 1406n = 2811.5
1406n BC → AD 1407t = 2812.5
```

The invalid display does not preserve equal component spans once the `.5` phase is opened. It also changes the AD-side internal distance from `.5` to `1.5` years. Therefore the paired Mirror display is not arbitrary: it is mathematically required by the phase-state.

### D.35.3 File_69 additional Machine Guards — verbatim registration

Machine Guard [FILE_69 FIRST-TOLEDOT LITERARY-HINGE / DAY-ASSIGNMENT]:
Genesis 2:4 stands after Genesis 2:1–3 and opens a recapitulated Day-6 human-formation narrative. The literary hinge may be compared with `6+1|6+1`, but the heading itself must not be assigned Day 6, Day 7, Day 13, Day 14, or a BC date. The Day-13/Day-14 recovery reading remains typological and dependency-controlled.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 TOLEDOT HEADING / CHRONOLOGICAL NODE NON-COLLAPSE]:
A Genesis *toledot* formula is a literary heading. A date attached to its named subject, first dated datum, father-death seam, wedding, call, or terminal event is not the date of the formula. `2026 BC` is Isaac's wedding datum; `2006 BC` is Jacob's birth; `1876 BC` is Jacob's Entry / *shemot* threshold.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 SONS-OF-NOAH / SHEM HEADING NON-COLLAPSE]:
Genesis 10:1 heads the *toledot* of Noah's sons and the Table of Nations. Genesis 11:10 heads Shem's individual genealogy. The two headings may form successive Shem-bearing frames but must not be treated as one formula or one literary unit.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 SHEM DEATH TEXTUAL-DATUM / DERIVED-DATE FIREWALL]:
Genesis 11:10 supplies Shem's age and the genealogy leading to Terah; it does not explicitly state Shem's death date. `2146/2086/2016/1956 BC` are derived regular-chronology labels under declared variants and must not be quoted as direct *toledot* wording.

Genesis 5:3 assigns `130` to Adam at Seth's birth; Genesis 5:6 assigns `105` to Seth at Enosh's birth. File_69 may use `130=13×10` as the bounded local ingress and record `105=7×15`, but the `235`, `1890`, and `1715` secondary branches remain outside the main proof unless separately reopened.

Machine Guard [FILE_69 UNSHIFTED TERAH CORE / `−215` NON-EXECUTION]:

## D.37 File_70 Jacob Burial-Day / Day–Year Calibration Amendment

Registered August 23 2026 after renewed author-invoked Finalization, Independent Pass 2 Verification, and the combined pressure test. This entry is **additive** to §D.36: the August 22 capstone registration, its `30` source guards, and its two reciprocal guards remain unchanged. Section D.37 registers only the eight amendment guards and the bounded review disposition.

### D.37.1 Amendment hierarchy and proof-burden routing

1. **Primary theorem — death-centered bilateral `70+7`:**

   ```text
   1936 ──7──▶ 1929 ──70──▶ [1859] ──70──▶ 1789 ──7──▶ 1782 BC
                              Jacob dies
   ```

   The birth-side recurrence is `2006→1936→1929 BC=70+7`. Genesis 50 supplies the numbered `70`-day Egyptian mourning station and `7`-day Atad station; it does not number the journey as part of one uninterrupted `77`-day interval.

2. **Secondary module — Haran month / embalming `30+40`:**

   ```text
   1959 ─30─ 1929 ─30─ 1899 ─40─ 1859 ─40─ 1819 BC
   ```

   The `1899 BC` meeting coordinate is conditional on the declared schematic thirty-day month and the inclusive mourning-horizon reading. It remains a File_70-local generated seam, not a historical event or Primary anchor.

3. **Canonical operator controls — compact and subordinate:** Ezekiel explicitly enacts `390+40` days as years; Daniel 9 supplies `70×7=490`; Daniel 12 supplies the coordinated `1260/1290` half-week field; and `2006→606→536 BC=1470=10×147` is tenfold factor regrouping, not event-order replication.

4. **Parent proof burdens remain unchanged:** Files 01, 04, 05, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17, 18, 61, 63, and 68 retain their chronology, calendar, operator, and node-class controls. Files 69 and 29 retain their already registered reciprocal capstone guards. File_70 does not retroject its generated projections into those files.

5. **Operator ceiling:** the registered Jacob/Ezekiel/Daniel evidence does not authorize indiscriminate conversion of biblical days, months, weeks, mourning periods, or ritual durations into years.

## D.38 Nested `±2 / ±720` Three-Gear Control Correction

Registered August 25 2026 after the author identified that the inherited `+2 local rail / +720 scale-state` firewall preserved unit separation but exposed only one side of each wheel. This correction is **modal-state and Machine-Guard work, not an arithmetic revision**. It supersedes the one-sided operative wording in §31.156, §32N, §32U, §D.10, and the corresponding prior source guards while retaining those migrated passages as source history.

### D.38.1 Controlling topology

File_11 remains the absolute Three-Gear controller:

```text
Absolute notation
Gear 1 / Japheth | Gear 2 / Shem | Gear 3 / Ham
       +0        |       +2      |       +4
```

Measured from the Gear-2 / Shem center, the identical wheel is:

```text
Shem-centered local notation
Gear 1 | Gear 2 | Gear 3
  −2   |    0   |   +2
```

Under the SKL `360:1` scalar, it dilates exactly to:

```text
SKL Gear notation
SKL Gear 1 | SKL Gear 2 | SKL Gear 3
    −720   |      0     |    +720
```

The three presentations are one topology in different coordinates and units:

| State | Coordinate form | Unit / function | Control |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Absolute ordinary wheel | `+0/+2/+4` | ordinary years from Gear 1 | File_11 controlling notation |
| Shem-centered ordinary wheel | `−2/0/+2` | ordinary-year local teeth about Gear 2 | exact recentering of File_11 |
| SKL macro wheel | `−720/0/+720` | years-of-years / cosmic teeth | `360:1` dilation of the centered local wheel |
| Second-order traversal | `720×360=259200` | full macro-to-outer scale traversal | distinct scale state; not another local tooth |
| Orthogonal apparent rail | `±30` | applied-last Apparent Mode | not part of either Three-Gear wheel |
| Maximum/container state | `7200=6480+720` | File_21 four-state maximum | distinct from local `±2` and macro `±720` |

Therefore:

```text
2×360 = 720
720×360 = 259200
```

The first equation defines the macro tooth. The second defines a further traversal; it does not collapse `2`, `720`, and `259200` into one unit.

### D.38.2 Nested local teeth within macro gears

For a declared macro member `M`, the optional local subrail is:

```text
M−2 | M | M+2.
```

For a center coordinate `D`, the complete nested matrix is:

| Macro member | Macro coordinate | Nested ordinary-year subrail |
|---|---:|---:|
| SKL Gear 1 | `D−720` | `D−722 / D−720 / D−718` |
| SKL Gear 2 | `D` | `D−2 / D / D+2` |
| SKL Gear 3 | `D+720` | `D+718 / D+720 / D+722` |

BC display order may reverse which coordinate is visually earlier or later; the algebraic state labels remain centered on the declared `D`. For example, with `D=262086 BC`, the BC-facing center wheel may be displayed `262088/262086/262084 BC`; at the `D+720=262806 BC` macro member, the nested wheel may be displayed `262808/262806/262804 BC`. A local subrail is admitted only where the source file explicitly opens it for phase, Mirror, or rail-closure work. It creates no new ancient source datum, raw table row, proof witness, target hit, or Primary anchor.

### D.38.3 Orthogonal `+30` rail and the `690/720/750` field

The `+30 Apparent Mode` remains orthogonal and is applied after selection of the macro and local Gear states. For a declared adjacent-macro comparison in which one endpoint occupies the `+30` rail:

```text
720−30 = 690
720      = 720
720+30 = 750
```

and:

```text
690×25/23 = 750.
```

This is a bounded state comparison, not an uncertainty interval, tolerance, or general permission to choose arbitrary `±30` endpoint combinations.

### D.38.4 Mirror orientation and source-state identity

Mirror inversion may reverse the **Gear-facing order** because left/right, earlier/later, or Gear-1/Gear-3 direction is viewed from the opposite side. It does not:

- rename the underlying coordinate states;
- exchange the units `2`, `720`, and `7200`;
- convert the `+30` rail into a Gear tooth;
- alter the governing civil or phase-resolved Mirror formula;
- promote a derived nested coordinate into source data.

Where phase suffixes are active, they must still be paired under the controlling File_09 / File_12 suffix-inversion rules. The nested-wheel correction supplies coordinate teeth; it does not introduce a new Mirror arithmetic.

### D.38.5 Superseding repository guard

Machine Guard [NESTED `±2 / ±720` THREE-GEAR CONTROL]:
The ordinary Three-Gear field is `+0/+2/+4` in absolute notation and `−2/0/+2` when centered on Gear 2 / Shem. Under the SKL `360:1` dilation, the centered field is `−720/0/+720`. The local and macro wheels are homologous scale states but non-interchangeable units. Each admitted macro member may carry a nested local `−2/0/+2` subrail only when explicitly opened. Existing `+2` and `+720` rows are selected-member displays, not the complete wheel. The `+30 Apparent Mode` is orthogonal and applied last; `7200=6480+720` remains a distinct maximum/container state. Mirror inversion may reverse Gear-facing order, but it does not rename source-coordinate states, alter suffix rules, or collapse local, macro, and maximum units.

### D.43.3 Verbatim File_18 Machine Guards

Machine Guard [FILE_18 LXX REGULAR / CUMULATIVE]:

The main LXX reconstruction retains the attested/transmitted LXX lifespan `753` (Genesis 5:31) with restored operative begetting age `182` in both Regular and Cumulative execution. `753` itself is not reconstructed; its retention as the preferred original LXX lifespan is the author-adopted textual/structural judgment of §4A.1. The remainder `571 = 753 − 182` is calculated, not an identified manuscript reading. No surviving joint `182/753` attestation has been identified in the reviewed sources; this qualification concerns the combined biography, not the attestation of `753`. Regular begetting paths and cumulative lifespan paths remain distinct methods, but Lamech's selected lifespan no longer changes between them. LXX use of MT `777` is an appendix overlay confined to Appendix B and appendix-routed downstream comparisons; its cumulative overlay is row-local and must not propagate into the `753` chain. MT `777` and Nahor's controlled LXX `208` remain unchanged. The transmitted `188` is non-operative: its possible Key-of-23 origin is a structural/transmission hypothesis, not an admitted main-chain variant.

Machine Guard [FILE_18 LAMECH KEY / TRANSMISSION]:

File_18 §4A records exact arithmetic within declared chronological states and an author-favored Key-of-23 crossover hypothesis. Neither harmonic compatibility nor a generated `+6` endpoint proves a historical scribal sequence or a joint `182/753` manuscript. The continuous LXX `3336 → 3267 → 2738 BC` comparison is cross-Gear (`69 + 529 = 598`), not a replacement of Shem's `600`-year lifespan. The MT `3127 → 3058 BC = 69` and `3056 → 2458 BC = 598` are separately located intervals; their relationship must not be presented as one attested continuous MT three-node partition. Gain-transfer, anchored expansion, and whole-biography translation must be distinguished. `188`-based comparison outputs remain non-operative, and applying `188` to the otherwise unchanged birth chain does not preserve its `1656` total.

## D.44 September 13 2026 Pre-Pro Canonical Refresh

### D.44.1 Bounded Terah-inclusive localized `−33` scope

Current operative rule: the localized `−33` corridor begins at **Terah** and continues through Abraham→Jacob/Levi. It remains bounded before Noah/Shem. This supersedes earlier current-control wording that named Abraham–Levi as the entire operative scope; historical notes describing Payne's Abraham–Levi usage remain historical/source commentary.

Registered Terah states:

```text
+60:    2296→2091 BC
base:   2236→2031 BC
−33:    2203→1998 BC
```

The three Terah biography states generate age-`70` cross-state outputs `2226 / 2166 / 2133 BC`; they are **not** three biological sibling births in one biography. Under the selected `+60` Terah biography, `2226 BC` is the firstborn threshold and `2166 BC` is Abram's ordinary biological birth at Terah age `130`. The `2133 BC` member remains a localized `−33` positional companion and is not assigned to Nahor.

A separate qualified sibling model may record `2106 BC` as a **candidate** Nahor birth, giving `2226→2166→2106 = 60+60 = 120`. Supporting comparisons include `2558−2166=392=8×49`, `2456−2106=350`, `2106−1406=700`, and the SP Terah dual-lock `145+60=205`. The `2106 BC` identification is structural inference only: it is not textual datum, not a Primary anchor, and must not be retrojected into source tables.

The LXX `33 + 397` interpretation itself is not repository-original. J. Barton Payne independently uses the same `33`-years-in-Palestine / `397`-years-in-Egypt logic and dates the LXX descent to `1843 BC`; the distinctive Unified Chronology claim is the bounded extension of localized `−33` through Terah while stopping before Noah/Shem.

Key bounded relations:

```text
2458−1998 = 460 = 20×23
1998−1446 = 552 = 24×23
3654−1998 = 1656 = 72×23
4114−1998 = 2116 = 46² = 92×23
4114−3056 = 3056−1998 = 1058 = 23×46
```

Qualified composite: `2263→2058=205`, with localized Abraham Call at `2058`. Net `+27` is not a fourth sibling position and not a global operator. `File_00` controls repository scope; `File_60` controls the detailed lattice; `File_01` carries the bounded Abraham/Terah dependency pointer; Files 04/16/61 retain downstream subset use.

### D.44.2 Shem `2558/2556` role-state clarification

- `2558 BC` = Shem Gear-2 triad / biological birth reference.
- `2556 BC` = elected/covenant-line Gear-1 station, not a second biological birth.
- File_47's cross-state construction intentionally uses `2558→2456=102` in Column A and `3058→2556=502` in Column B, giving `322+732=1054` against literal `3058→2006=1052`; the extra `2` is the explicit state overlap `2558→2556`, not elapsed historical time.
- The co-shift `322/730` reading remains separately valid; neither reading corrects the other.

### D.44.3 Cumulative seven-year object

The cumulative chronology's primary local object is a **seven-year week**. Whole-year decompositions are state-qualified:

```text
2+3+2 = 7   balanced/default
2+2+3 = 7   Gear-dependent
3+2+2 = 7   Gear-dependent
2+1+1+1+2 = 7   superimposed whole-year boundary refinement
```

The `.5` Tishri/Nisan phase is separate. Do not treat these partitions as simultaneously applied operators or infer independently precise `±2` at every later patriarch.

### D.44.4 Aaron/Moses phase and lifespan guard

Aaron dies **before** Moses. Aaron's source lifespan remains `123`; Moses' remains `120`. Preferred phase handling: Aaron birth `1530t` with `1529n` allowed; Moses birth `1526n` with `1527t` allowed. Aaron dies on Av 1 in the repository's 1407 BC terminal chronology and is schematically represented about `1407t`; Moses dies later in the winter phase of 1406 BC and is schematically about `1406n`. West-Jordan crossing remains Nisan 10, `1406n`. The `3.5` relation is a phase-track separation, not a `121/124` lifespan variant.

### D.44.5 File_18 LXX post-Cainan source-table correction

File_18 §4.2 controls the corrected LXX Regular-Minimum triplet:

```text
Peleg  2492→2153 BC   lifespan 339   begetting 130
Reu    2362→2023 BC   lifespan 339   begetting 132
Serug  2230→1900 BC   lifespan 330   begetting 130
```

The former `239/239/230` lifespans and `2253/2123/2000 BC` deaths in that LXX table were copy errors, not variants. Births and begetting ages remain unchanged. SP rows retaining `239/239/230` are not altered by this LXX correction. The File_18 Lamech `182/753` hierarchy remains governed separately by §D.43.

### D.44.6 Already-applied Shelah control

No new Shelah patch is required in this refresh. Current File_09 already carries the terminal seam `4398–4391 → 4358–4351 BC`, the `7+33 / 33+7` reversal, `7+483+7`, and completed `7+490+7` field. This entry records that the previously deferred Shelah correction is already canonical in the supplied File_09 source.

### D.44.7 Verification / publication boundary

This is a bounded author-directed canonical refresh before the next Pro research chat. It changes only the named source-table cells, scope/role-state wording, and control registration. It does not claim a new whole-repository pressure test, change the current research dependency rank `49/66`, alter the 17 residual directions, execute the next File_62 audit, or revise the 80-stage audit archive.
